## Community card readability — local, 2026-10-31

Within the approved Profile → review → Community journey, publication cards used 9–11px for category, author and date labels, an 11px primary action, and a 10px spoiler disclosure with a 34px minimum height. Community labels now follow the 12px label role, the primary action follows the 14px control role, and spoiler disclosure uses 12px text with a 44px target.

The new 320×700 browser check first reproduced the 10px eyebrow, then passes 1/1 in dark and light themes with axe WCAG 2.2 AA, card-overflow and touch-target assertions, and spoiler reveal behavior. The existing populated-review journey passes 1/1, including dark and light mobile states, archive return and guest sign-in return. `@consumit/web` typecheck passes. Web lint still reports the existing `global-error.tsx:18` raw anchor to `/`; `tests/experience` is excluded by the repository lint config. Impeccable detect ran once on the global Taste stylesheet and still reports other advisory type, color and radius deviations outside this card-label adjustment. Inspected captures: `.impeccable/review/review-journey/community-following-320x700.png` and `community-following-320x700-light.png`. This verifies the tested phone presentation; other Community states and the whole-app audit remain open.

## Unified provider egress: resolution fixes staged — local + VPS, 2026-10-31

Member job: restore reliable playback for Wecollege, VidBolt, EmbedMaster and NontonFlix. Diagnosis across controlled egresses established two independent upstream blocks on VidBolt resolution and a stale-host defect on Wecollege, so one egress change alone would not restore all four.

Canary evidence (residential egress, real adapter, production `node:https` transport) separated the causes: `vidbolt.xyz` rejects a missing or literal-`node` User-Agent with 403 from every egress, and a browser User-Agent passes from residential egress but remains 403 from Cloudflare/VPS egress. The `vidbolt` adapter had omitted the header; it now sends the shared browser `userAgent` on the resolution request. This header fix is necessary but does not lift the IP-class block, so VidBolt (and Wecollege's 429 from Cloudflare) still require the deferred residential egress gateway before members are served. Host evidence across nine titles yields live sources only from `img.rousav.tech` (5 movies) and `proxy.vidbolt.xyz` (3 series); `img1.hscow.com` and `img.animecurx.tech` returned none, so only `proxy.vidbolt.xyz` was widened.

The VidBolt browser-UA fix and the WeCollege `moon.goldenkite.top` recipe were added behind tests in `provider-sdk`/`media-relay` (608 provider-sdk, 87 media-relay, 58 focused checks pass). The roster at `/opt/consumit/infra/secrets/providers.production.json` and `PLAYBACK_RELAY_PROVIDERS` in `.env.production` were staged additively with backups: both WeCollege rows gained `moon.goldenkite.top` and the VidBolt row gained `proxy.vidbolt.xyz`, with file mode/ownership preserved and both files re-parsed clean. The media-relay Worker was deliberately not redeployed — its checked-in deploy maps are empty (`{}`) or stale and would regress production, and the media leg has no effect until egress lands; reconciling those maps belongs with the gateway slice. No provider serves members yet: this slice lands prerequisites only, and no commit was made over the 366 pre-existing working-tree files.

`BecauseYouWatchedRail` previously kept account-scoped state after the session changed. A second member could see the prior member's Undo action, and a failed history read could reveal the prior recommendations. The rail now clears old member results and actions as the new member's first read settles; same-member refresh failures retain that member's known data and retry path.

Two component regressions first reproduced the cross-member Undo leak and stale-result exposure after a failed new-member read. They now pass along with the full rail suite (7/7); the failing new-member read shows Retry, and retry restores only that member's suggestions. The existing Home dismissal/retry browser journey passes 1/1 at 320×720 with axe WCAG 2.2 AA and no horizontal overflow. The no-match empty state now provides direct category routes to Movies, Series, and Anime. The added Discover browser case checks these routes at 320×720, validates 44px targets, axe WCAG 2.2 AA, and no horizontal overflow, then follows Browse anime. Web typecheck and targeted ESLint pass, and rendered states are inspected after the browser run. These are deterministic component and browser fixture results, not production account-switch persistence evidence.

## Blocking removes the current public projection — local, 2026-10-30

The approved Profile → review → Community browser seam covers a failed block write, focused retry, successful author block, refresh to the unavailable state, and focus. It also blocks a commenter from an open review, removes their loaded row, keeps the review and composer usable, announces the change with a 44px settings link, and confirms the row stays hidden after reload. # Delivery strategy

## Recovery code verification and Remember me — local, 2026-10-31

The auth contract now supports a separate recovery verification step. A successful code consumes the challenge and sets a short-lived HttpOnly recovery grant; the password screen can no longer be reached through the flow until verification succeeds. Completing the reset consumes the grant and revokes existing sessions. The old combined confirmation endpoint remains compatible. Sign-in now accepts optional `rememberMe`; the browser-session choice omits cookie `Max-Age`, while clients that omit the field keep current persistence behavior.

The standalone `consumit-web` Storybook flow now waits for its verification callback, uses `204817` as a local-only sample code, removes code editing from the reset screen, and includes the approved checkbox in sign-in. Its API callbacks are not wired to `/v1` yet. No tests or build were run in this implementation pass.

## Inline signup recovery and phone form bounds — local, 2026-10-28

Member job: continue creating an account after a failed request or a return to earlier details. Explicit step transitions now focus Email, Username or Verification code. Registration, confirmation and resend share synchronous request guards; pending fieldsets and navigation actions lock the submitted draft. Failed operations retain fields and focus the error. Accepted resend clears the previous code and focuses code entry. Use a different email now lives in the main flow on phones and larger screens, rather than only the hidden phone aside.

After an accepted registration, returning to details exposes Return to pending verification. It restores the original email, password, username and display name from component memory, clears partial code entry and resumes without another registration request. The browser journey verifies the original profile name through the member API and continues through onboarding to the original destination. This restores an existing request; transferring an already-reserved username to a corrected email remains open under the current identity contract.

Visual review caught a clipped long-address form despite a passing document-width assertion: the fieldset's intrinsic minimum width stretched the controls beyond the phone. Signup fieldsets now permit shrinking with `min-w-0`, and the 320px regression requires the actual input and both action bounds to remain inside the viewport gutters. The updated long-address and failed-confirmation phone captures were inspected under `.impeccable/review/account-verification/`.

The combined targeted run passed all thirteen browser checks (1.6 minutes): the previous ten plus phone step/pending recovery, pending-registration restoration and inline resend/confirmation failure/retry. Each new behavior first had a failing browser check. All twenty-three existing auth/return-path unit checks passed; web typecheck passed after the browser server stopped; changed auth source/test-source lint passed without output. Root ESLint does not configure the experience spec files, so its ignored-file result is not counted as a lint pass for the browser spec.

This is local deterministic HTTP/browser evidence, without deployment or live email certification. Inline expired-code recovery, cooldown guidance, changing a reserved identity's email, remaining sign-in validation and the wider route ledger remain open. The full-app goal stays active.

## Signup paths, verification recovery and stable submission — local, 2026-10-27

Member job: choose an account path, verify an email and continue the original task. Sign-in/signup switching and the unverified-email action now retain the validated destination; the signup sign-in link has a 44px target. Standalone verification carries that destination through onboarding and Skip. Verification and resend share a synchronous pending guard and one action state; fields lock while either is pending, errors retain entered details and receive focus. Resend validates the email independently of the code, retains a failed draft, and clears/focuses code entry on acceptance. Rejected-code feedback offers signup restart with the same destination.

The real expiry journey advances only the in-memory identity fixture clock by eleven minutes, receives the actual invalid/expired response, restarts signup with the same email/username, verifies a fresh code and reaches the original URL. The resend journey crosses the server's request cooldown through the fixture clock, recovers a controlled delivery failure and verifies the actual newly issued code. These checks do not wait in real time or change production identity behavior.

That restart journey exposed a second defect: username feedback was inserted on blur and shifted the submit button by 23.5px during the first pointer click. The captured browser trace showed no registration request. A smaller geometry regression reproduced the movement; reserving one persistent live feedback region keeps the button within the one-pixel stability gate. The original expiry/restart pointer flow now passes as well. No production logging or throwaway instrumentation remains.

The final targeted run passed all ten browser checks together (1.7 minutes): five verification/switching/stability journeys, four password-recovery journeys and the existing guest-signup/device-choice/consent/destination journey. All twenty-three existing auth/return-path unit checks and changed auth/test/verify-route lint passed. Web typecheck passed after the final browser server stopped. Phone retry, expiry and resend captures under `.impeccable/review/account-verification/` were inspected.

This remains deterministic local HTTP/browser evidence, without deployment, live email or physical-device certification. The unverified-login branch is exercised with a controlled response; actual registration verification uses in-memory HTTP services. Inline signup step focus, pending/editability and mobile email correction remain in the whole-app ledger alongside the remaining routes. A later resend-guidance checkpoint records the one-minute email expectation and its phone accessibility scan.

Web typecheck passed with the development server stopped, and changed auth/test/route-source lint passed without output. The rendered light success confirmation passes the 4.5:1 contrast gate after replacing the fixed pale text color.

This remains deterministic local HTTP/browser evidence, without deployment, live email or physical-device certification. Signup/verification destination switching, expiry/resend behavior and the remaining whole-app ledger remain open; the earlier collection checkpoints retain their separate evidence.

Undo is a single most-recent title removal during the current editor visit, rather than durable change history. Larger/maximum-capacity editing, chapter deletion/reassignment, initial server archive enrichment and the remaining whole-app routes stay open.

Completion focuses the first new visible record in the chosen sort order, or the nearby completion status when the search hides the new records.

## Active product strategy — watch to conversation (2026-10-26)

Reliable, source-agnostic playback is Consumit's trust foundation. The product bet is to make a watch matter afterward: a member can keep a private record, deliberately express a reaction, respond to another member's published work, and find a next watch through shared taste. This is a strategic hypothesis, not a claim that these connections improve retention.

The post-watch handoff, Taste Publications, Community, and focused title recommendation card are implemented and active locally and in production. Six additive migrations, including `TasteRecommendation`, were applied to production on 2026-10-26. The product API and web are live; the playback and compatible legacy services retain their previous images and routing. Build on the existing profile, list, review, reply, and moderation features rather than duplicating them.

### Sequence

1. **Connect the watch to existing taste actions — implemented in the workspace.** Completed movie playback offers optional rating, Diary, and review actions. A rating can stand alone; dismissing the prompt remains easy. Episode playback keeps the compact continuation action and never opens the full review composer. A completed watch remains private History unless the member deliberately records or publishes something. Provider frames do not count as completion.
2. **Evaluate a focused title recommendation card — implemented locally and in production.** A member can send a title card to someone they follow; the recipient can Save, Already seen, or Not interested. Save and Already seen use existing private library records; Not interested updates the existing private dismissal record. The card carries no message and does not create a private-message surface. Production uses the existing split playback service; its provider routing was not changed for this feature release.
3. **Maintain Anime's weekly routine.** Episode tracking, Home's New this week, and optional release reminders already provide the baseline routine. Any external tracker integration remains a separate feasibility decision and is not part of this implementation.

### Aggregate evaluation

Calculate baselines from retained product records after the compatible release is available: completed watches followed within seven days by an intentional rating, Diary entry, or review; public reviews receiving at least one reply within seven days; and contributors who watch or publish again within seven days. Report aggregate counts only; do not add identity- or title-linked Google Analytics events. Set targets after the baseline and describe relationships as associations, not causal retention effects.

Initial production snapshot (2026-10-26 15:54 UTC): among 10 completed watches with a full seven-day observation window (completed 2026-10-16 01:24–2026-10-19 15:53 UTC), 0 were followed by a native rating, Diary entry, or non-removed native review for the same member and title within seven days. There were 0 eligible public reviews old enough to measure replies, so that rate is unavailable rather than 0%. Among 4 members whose earliest retained watch or public publication activity was at least seven days old, 3 had another completed watch or public publication within seven days (3/4). Publication activity uses public lists, visible reviews, and published editions; this is a first-retained-activity cohort, not all-time contributor history. The watch record history currently spans 2026-10-16–2026-10-26. These are small, early descriptive aggregates; the post-watch and recommendation release activated on 2026-10-26, so this snapshot is not an estimate of its effect. No targets are set. Recheck after a larger mature cohort accumulates.

Keep playback success and accurate completion as guardrails. A watch remains private by default, reactions are optional with audience behavior explained, and episodes are not interrupted by a full review form. The feature is live; outcome measurement remains preliminary and the release has no post-activation seven-day cohort yet. See the production activation and measurement record below.

## Community and taste production rollout attempt — 2026-10-26

A verified full pgBackRest backup and stanza check completed before activation. Prisma reported six pending migrations; all six applied successfully: `20261021170000_connected_member_experience`, `20261022180000_taste_publications`, `20261022190000_collection_genres`, `20261022200000_independent_social_preferences`, `20261024190000_subtitle_correction_scope`, and `20261026120000_taste_recommendations`.

The candidate API image repeatedly failed its health check with `Production playback requires Cloudflare-only provider execution.` The merged production environment did not provide the required `PROVIDER_EXECUTION_MODE=cloudflare-only` runtime setting. Activating `compose.provider-edge.yaml` would also change API legacy, playback, and release-worker configuration; its own deployment note requires reviewed route evidence, scoped R2 access, verified quotas, and subtitle lifecycle rules. The existing Cloudflare provider audits document unresolved upstream reachability and route-map discrepancies, so that overlay was not enabled as a workaround.

The API and web containers were restored to `consumit-api:rebuild-20261024-streamvault-native-v3` and `consumit-web:rebuild-20261024-provider-reliability-v1`; both are healthy. Production homepage and `/health/ready` return HTTP 200. `/community?mode=discover` and `/v1/taste/community?mode=discover` remain HTTP 404. Production schema is six migrations ahead of the active API/web images; no reverse migration was attempted.

The 404 responses and migration lag above describe the rollback checkpoint; the later activation is recorded below.

## Production startup fix and activation — 2026-10-26

The failed candidate was the split product API (`APPLICATION_MODE=product`), which forwards playback attempts to the separate playback service. `loadEnv` incorrectly required Cloudflare-only provider execution for every production process with playback enabled, even though the product API does not instantiate a provider runtime. This was a process-ownership validation bug, not a missing production playback route. The validation now enforces Cloudflare-only execution and its Resolver Worker in processes that actually execute providers. A Subtitle Worker is optional; the accepted initial subtitle architecture continues to use playback's existing volume. The unverified subtitle Worker and provider-edge Compose overlay were not enabled.

Built `consumit-api:rebuild-20261026-community-mode-fix-v1` from the community release source and activated it with `consumit-web:web-20261026-community-v1`. Only the product API and web were recreated, without dependencies. Production API and web are healthy. Public `/`, `/community?mode=discover`, `/notifications`, `/watch/movie/550`, `/health/ready`, and `/v1/taste/community?mode=discover` returned HTTP 200; unauthenticated `/v1/taste/recommendations` returned HTTP 401. The existing playback image `consumit-playback:rebuild-20261024-streamvault-native-v3` and API legacy image `consumit-api:rebuild-20261024-streamvault-native-v3` remain healthy and unchanged. No playback routing, provider policy, subtitle-worker deployment, database migration, or analytics event was added in this follow-up. The prior API/web tags and saved rollback Compose override remain available.

## Evion Cloudflare lookup block — production, 2026-10-25

An isolated Cloudflare diagnostic Worker ran the exact Evion adapter for S1E1 (TMDB 1399). `api.bingr.one` returned HTTP 403 with Cloudflare's `Attention Required!` page at Madrid, Paris, and Virginia; no media-host request followed. The same API endpoint had resolved from the production API container, but the owner requires provider lookup and media to stay on Cloudflare. No VPS fallback is included. Evion needs the upstream operator to allow Cloudflare Resolver egress or expose an endpoint that accepts it. A separate Worker diagnostic found WeCollege's API returns 429 from Cloudflare. The legacy API lookup exception has not been changed: a guarded reconciliation stopped after finding that three active Worker route policy maps also differ from `.env.production`. No production file or service was changed; preserve the active Worker maps before applying the Cloudflare-only route list. No provider rows or media hosts were widened.

Local movie first-frame/seek checks passed five of seven requested rows; an isolated production-environment candidate passed both movie and episode for Mapple/VidBolt, but the subsequent live application recheck passed only VidBolt's episode. These are diagnostic results, not repairs or broad acceptance. The [priority-provider record](./audits/priority-provider-production-2026-10-24.md) captures effective eligibility, missing policies, native/Worker comparisons and next repair boundaries. No production settings, images, provider overrides or Worker deployments changed.

## Playback transport IPv4 preference - production, 2026-10-23

Provider resolution now prefers IPv4 when DNS publishes AAAA before A. The production VPS has no working IPv6 route, while Cloudflare-fronted VidBolt, Netoda and StreamVault hosts publish IPv6 first; `createMediaTransport` pinned that unreachable address and requests timed out until the provider circuit opened. The transport now selects the first IPv4 address and falls back to IPv6 only for IPv6-only hosts. Regression test `pins IPv4 when DNS publishes AAAA before A` covers the ordering.

Release `rebuild-20261023-ipv4-clean-v4` is live for API, playback, API legacy and media egress, and `compose.rebuild.pins.json` matches. Production checks after deployment: Netoda resolved movie 13 and played Arcane S1E1, VidBolt played movie 155, StreamVault played movie 603, and Mapple played movie 550. A ten-title resolution sweep passed Netoda 10/10 and VidBolt 9/10. The remaining VidBolt miss used `proxy.vidbolt.xyz`, which is not in the production host policy. StreamVault still answers `PLAYBACK_UNAVAILABLE` when its only direct source is header-locked, and the reported wrong-title case is not reproduced. Both host policies stay unchanged pending a separate playback acceptance.

## Provider route diagnosis - local and production, 2026-10-23

Added `scripts/diagnose-provider-session.mjs` to exercise the public playback session route and browser media path. It keeps source URLs and grants out of output, checks decoded first frame and a seek/resume in Chrome, and reports the configured delivery tier. This is the correct loop for Direct and Relay rows; `scripts/diagnose-providers.ts` resolves raw adapter candidates and tests them browser-direct, so a Relay-only source can fail that older check by design.

Against the local gateway, WeCollege Miami returned a Relay session for Fight Club (TMDB 550) and Breaking Bad S1E1 (TMDB 1396), decoded at 1920×1080, and passed first-frame plus seek/resume on both. Production previously returned `PLAYBACK_UNAVAILABLE` for both references. The former WeCollege API lookup exception is retired; all provider lookups now use Cloudflare. Recheck the Worker 429 category and route-specific backoff on the next production canary. No host policy was widened by this implementation.

VidBolt's effective production roster is enabled as Relay. Samples fluctuated: an earlier and latest production session pass decoded and sought both references; an intervening bounded pass returned `PLAYBACK_UNAVAILABLE` for both and health read degraded. The same-day ten-title sweep was 9/10, with its miss resolving only on `proxy.vidbolt.xyz`, which remains outside the production API and Worker host policies. The local relay map already allowed the two production-tested hosts, but `providers.local.json` had no VidBolt registration; a local row is now enabled with only those two hosts. After restarting the local API, both VidBolt movie 550 and Breaking Bad S1E1 decoded at 1920×1080 and passed first-frame plus seek/resume. WeCollege Miami passed the same post-restart checks for both references. The current local roster lists 31 movie, 30 series, and 20 anime entries; these are selectable configuration counts, not playback acceptance counts. Boise remains absent from the effective local roster because its saved Admin disable is intentional per the 2026-10-03 delivery record. Neither fact supports enabling another VidBolt host.

Focused checks pass: 152 provider SDK tests, 70 API configured-provider/resolver/environment tests, and 6 Playback runtime-routing tests. The 1920×1080 local WeCollege browser checks pass. Production environment, roster, and Worker policy were not changed.

The broader local session sweep exercised every currently selectable Direct/Relay video row once with its configured probe reference where present: first-frame plus seek/resume passed for 11/25 movie rows, 11/24 series rows, and 7/17 anime rows. Twenty-five attempts returned no source, ten resolved but hit an HLS network error, and two native-media attempts failed in Chrome. The roster still reported `ok` for rows whose Relay requests returned 502, confirming that provider health needs separate lookup and browser delivery evidence. These are point-in-time samples; they do not establish provider-wide or long-session reliability. The ignored `tmp/provider-session-local-*-20261023.json` reports contain only provider/reference, public hostname, status, and playback outcome, not signed URLs or grants. ADR 0012 now defines the implemented route-version gate and browser evidence requirement. Production Worker policy must be generated and canaried before API rollout.

The session report isolated Relay Worker failures for StigStream movie/series, EmbedMaster movie/series, and Netoda series; their local API Relay maps match their configured media hosts, so no host was added based on this result. Worker tail showed StigStream timing out during upstream fetch at 10 seconds, while EmbedMaster and Netoda manifests rewrote successfully and their subsequent media fetches received upstream HTTP 502. VidLink's direct native response was blocked by Chrome ORB on `bcdn.hakunaymatata.com`; VidZen and PurStream returned Direct HLS sources but failed browser fetch/decode. The remaining no-source results require provider-specific upstream evidence before changing adapters or disabling rows.

## NontonFlix and EmbedMaster PROVE Relay — workspace integration, 2026-10-22

NontonFlix and EmbedMaster are now implemented as Relay adapters in the provider SDK, registered by the API and playback runtime, and listed as disabled rows in `infra/playback/providers.example.json`. `nontonflix` bootstraps NontonFlix's device cookie, calls its NDJSON session endpoint, falls back through its ranked providers and returns at most eight HLS sources on exactly `media.nontonflix.com`. `embedmaster` follows EmbedMaster's front page to `embdmstrplayer.com`, POSTs the player token, decodes the XOR configuration, reads its feed and resolver, and returns at most six direct HLS/native sources on exactly `perfectbussines.website`.

Relay now permits one credential: the NontonFlix media cookie, only for that provider, only on `media.nontonflix.com`, capped at 512 bytes. The API issuer, SDK source validation, grant codec and Worker each enforce the binding; cross-host hints stay empty, so the cookie cannot follow a child or unrelated destination. EmbedMaster needs no credential; it sends only the fixed `embdmstrplayer.com` Referer and Origin. The manifest rewriter accepts 4 MiB for both large playlists, with 6,000 references for NontonFlix and the existing 2,000 for EmbedMaster. EmbedMaster's HTML-labelled segment host is accepted only after MPEG-TS byte verification on `perfectbussines.website`.

Local acceptance used the rebuilt Docker web, API and a staging media-relay Worker. With `?provider=`, the real Consumit Player switched to each provider and played Fight Club 1080p for 40 seconds without a stall; both began playback within 15 seconds of opening the page, with no false startup failure. The tracked Worker example and production environment example list both providers with their exact media hosts, but production is not changed by this work: the VPS environment and deployed Worker still need the same `PLAYBACK_RELAY_PROVIDERS`/`RELAY_PROVIDERS` additions and a fresh Worker deployment, then the member rows. Neither provider has a production playback, seek, Safari/native-HLS or seven-day acceptance record. Keep both rows disabled until the production acceptance passes. Media Relay (74) and UI (282) suites pass, as do affected type and lint checks. See [ADR 0011](./adr/0011-provider-scoped-media-credentials.md) and the [relay trial runbook](./runbooks/worker-relay-trial.md).

On 2026-10-23, release `rebuild-20261023-mapple-nfx-em-v1` deployed the NontonFlix/EmbedMaster adapters, relay handling and Mapple provider together. Mapple is live and resolves Fight Club (TMDB 550) as HLS relay in about 2.2s. NontonFlix and EmbedMaster were **not** enabled: production VPS egress receives Cloudflare `Just a moment...` 403 challenges on both `nontonflix.com` and `embedmaster.link`, so their adapters return `unavailable` before any source is selected. Their member rows are disabled and their host entries were removed from `PLAYBACK_RELAY_PROVIDERS`; the code and Worker policy remain deployed for a future egress solution. Do not enable either row until a production VPS request passes Cloudflare and the full playback/seek acceptance is repeated.

### Release checklist — current canary

- [x] Capture and verify the annotated full database backup; preserve pre-release environment, pins and provider configuration snapshots.
- [x] Deploy app services, web and the reviewed media relay Worker; run production route smoke and relay CORS preflight.
- [x] Keep the playback-process canary at 1%; preserve the existing provider roster and host checks.
- [ ] Verify decoded playback, seeking/resume and sustained playback for a movie, series episode and anime episode against production sources.
- [ ] Review existing resolve/start metrics and stall/fatal/provider alerts over a sufficient canary window; compare against reference SLOs only with an adequate sample.
- [ ] Exercise physical Android/Chromecast, Safari/iPhone and the reported PC issue before marking those device gaps verified.
- [ ] For a full release rollback, reapply the saved Compose pins and environment so the retained prior API, playback, worker, gateway and web images are selected, then rerun production smoke. For a playback-process-only rollback, run `node scripts/rebuild-release.mjs rollback-playback` from the staged release checkout with the saved environment and pin file; restore provider configuration only if a future rollout changed it.

## End-to-end playback reliability pass — local, 2026-10-20

The local reliability pass fixed confirmed Watch issues in the provider relay, controls and recovery fixtures. The relay now applies the reviewed BingeBang manifest transform to its current rotated media host while retaining exact provider and host checks. The touch skip prompt now sits above the seek input, so Skip intro/recap taps reach the button. Player panels can use up to 84% of a short viewport for caption choices. Browser startup recovery checks now follow the current automatic `/v1/playback/attempts` route and server picker. The shared browser fixture server disables only its process-local rate limiter so independent browser cases do not consume one another's API quota; production rate limits remain enabled. Watch's batched anime episode metadata now keeps null/not-found results in its existing short cache, avoiding repeated provider fan-out for unavailable episode art.

The latest [31-case production provider recheck](./audits/provider-production-recheck-2026-10-20.md) recorded 17 cases with a source and first media prefix; that evidence does not establish a decoded frame or sustained playback. AuroraScreen, MovieNight and StreamingUnity still receive upstream HTTP 403 from both production egress paths; Rigel and Aether had no source in the sampled movie/series cases. Resolve/start success, startup latency and stall timing were not measured with a sufficient decoded-playback sample to assess the reference SLOs (resolve success above 99%, fresh resolve p95 below 1.5s, playback start above 98%). Physical Android, Safari/iPhone and the reported PC issue remain unverified.

The pre-existing local `bingebangymovies` enable/priority edit is preserved separately and is not release-ready: a live configured source could not be exercised here for a decoded frame, seek/resume and 60 seconds of sustained playback without a fatal media error. At the close of this Oct 20 pass, production provider settings and the 1% playback-process canary were unchanged; no deployment or database migration had yet been performed.

Local verification after the fixes: API tests pass 641/641, UI tests 279/279 and media-relay tests 67/67. API, web, UI and relay lint/type checks pass. The full 84-case browser playback run recorded 73 passes and 9 intentional skips; its two failures used the prior 62% panel-height assertion. After updating that assertion to the shipped 84% limit, the five-viewport panel case passed in both Chrome and Chrome-touch. The separate full web unit run passed 575 tests and failed four unrelated Admin provider-bench, profile-edit and GA4-navigation cases; those failures remain outside this playback slice.

### Release checklist — future rollout only

- [ ] Back up the current provider configuration and take the normal verified database backup before rollout.
- [ ] Keep the existing 1% playback-process canary. Smoke one movie, one series episode and one anime episode from exact title identity through resolve, decoded frame, seek/resume, and sustained playback.
- [ ] Exercise startup timeout, stalls, source expiry/renewal, fallback and exhaustion, cancellation, source switching, audio/captions and episode transitions; confirm play intent and progress survive recovery.
- [ ] Watch the existing resolve/start metrics, startup latency and stall/fatal/provider alerts through the canary. Compare with the documented SLOs only when the sample is large enough; record upstream blocks separately.
- [ ] Roll back by restoring the prior application release and provider configuration from the saved backup, then rerun the playback smoke checks. Do not promote BingeBang unless it passes the 60-second decoded-playback acceptance above.
- [ ] Run physical Android, Safari/iPhone and reported-PC checks before marking those device gaps verified.

## Security review hardening — gateway internal-route block and account sign-in lockout, 2026-10-20

A full security review of the identity, playback, relay, provider, and gateway surfaces found no critical or high holes: sessions are server-side hashed opaque tokens in dedicated Valkey with argon2id credentials, the media transport is SSRF-pinned behind exact host allowlists, relay grants are AES-GCM sealed with 30-minute expiry, admin routes carry hook plus per-handler authorization, and no secrets are committed. Two review gaps are now closed. The default production and development gateway configurations refuse every private `/v1/internal/` path with 404, keeping only the secret-checked `/v1/internal/animekai-pow` compute callback, matching the rebuild gateway's existing boundary; the nginx configurations pass `nginx -t` in the built gateway image. Password sign-in now counts wrong passwords per account in the authentication store (ten per rolling fifteen-minute window, keyed by peppered HMAC, incremented atomically in Valkey Lua), so a distributed spray cannot outpace the per-address credential limit; counting starts only for known accounts, unknown addresses still answer invalid credentials, a successful sign-in clears the budget, and the existing pending-registration guidance is unchanged. The shared identity contract, memory-store, and Valkey integration tests cover the lockout; API type check and lint pass. Accepted, unchanged trade-offs remain product decisions: guest playback resolves direct provider URLs that are shareable for their natural TTL, and DRM stays deferred per ADR 0002. A production database credential that appeared in a local commented environment line was removed from the untracked local file and should be rotated. Neon is now fully removed from local configuration at the owner's request: the stale Neon DATABASE_URL and forced-SSL DATABASE_SSL_MODE lines were deleted from the root environment file so plain Compose falls back to its local Postgres service, the Neon URL template was dropped from the API environment example, and stale Neon wording in local environment comments, Compose comments, and the backup script was rewritten; production keeps its own self-hosted database configuration, and historical audit records keep their original wording. A second pass with live probes against the running stack closed three more gaps: subtitle archive language matching now escapes provider-supplied codes before building a regular expression, so hostile upstream metadata cannot stall the event loop, with a regression case in the unit suite; the development gateway configuration mirrors the production edge by refusing private `/v1/internal/` paths except the secret-checked AnimeKai compute callback, and a live probe after reload confirmed the boundary because the local gateway mounts `consumit.dev.conf` rather than the previously hardened `consumit.conf`; and the subtitle discovery prepare origin guard fails closed when environment configuration is missing. Live probes also confirmed that unknown credentials answer a uniform 401, cross-site mutations answer 403, member and admin surfaces answer 401 without sessions, foreign origins receive no CORS allowance, and the web response carries its CSP, HSTS, nosniff, referrer and permissions headers; git history confirms no environment file was ever committed. One advisory remains unfixable: braces@3.0.3 in the eslint toolchain has no patched release and is not runtime-exposed. The per-account lockout keeps its known availability trade-off — a known address can be held for fifteen minutes by ten failures — as the accepted cost of bounding password spraying.

## Lists and public taste — implementation and verification, 2026-10-19

Lists are first-class ordered collections with public-by-default creation, per-list privacy, editorial detail pages, link sharing, authenticated save/remove actions, and a private Saved Lists archive that follows source edits and marks hidden/deleted lists unavailable. Profile identity is public with independent visibility controls for Favorites, Ratings, Reviews, Lists, Network, Likes, Tags, and Watchlist; Watched, Diary, History, active playback, progress, and account data stay private. A separate off-by-default preference powers a title-only Home trend rail from seven-day opted-in playback, deduplicated by member/title and gated at five contributing members. Existing profiles migrate to visible section defaults, existing list visibility is preserved, and profile location data is removed. Privacy and Terms copy, Product, Architecture, Context, Design, Components, and this delivery record describe the same behavior. Verification passed: all 32 migrations deployed to the isolated test database; a seeded legacy upgrade preserved private/public lists, backfilled saved-list snapshots, applied visible section defaults, and removed profile location; API database integration passed 30 tests, route tests 33, contracts 23, and web interactions 40. API, web, and contracts type checks pass, as does Prisma schema validation. The implementation checks used disposable storage. The separate local rollout is recorded below; managed Neon and production were not changed.

## Next-watch recommendation ranking and row — deployed, 2026-10-19

For your next watch now combines recommendations from the eight freshest completed or 95%-watched titles instead of exposing a seed selector, type filter, and sort menu. A recency-decayed reciprocal-rank fusion rewards suggestions repeated across watched titles; genre overlap breaks ties, and a light diversification pass avoids overfilling the row with the same format or genre. Watched, duplicate and privately dismissed titles remain excluded. The interface is a heading, concise watch-history provenance, and one shared poster row, with Undo/Reset appearing only after a dismissal. Updated product, design, and component references describe the bounded catalog-derived signals without claiming Taste Match. The release passed full workspace checks, the Impeccable detector reported no findings, and post-deploy API, playback, boundary, public-site and route checks passed. The [production audit](./audits/full-workspace-deployment-2026-10-19.md) records the rollout and remaining acceptance limits.

## Unified player deck — locally complete, 2026-10-18

The approved member job is controlling and continuing an exact movie or episode through one understandable player deck. The main browser suite passes 24 scenarios with four intentional per-device skips; the supplementary run passes 11 with one intentional skip, covering decoded-frame captures, phone seek targets, keyboard/touch seeking, focus/auto-hide, caption-preparation failure and Anime SUB/DUB navigation (`tmp/player-deck-final-behavior-verified.log`). The independent follow-up returns Ship for the three findings reviewed, with all three resolved (`tmp/player-deck-finish-review.md`); it is not a whole-surface certification. The final desktop/touch source-selection regression also passes four tests in 36.1 seconds, covering paused switching, Auto without reload, roster retry and failed switching retaining decoded media (`tmp/player-deck-source-regression-verified.log`). Its test-only selector/focus and eager-roster fixture updates introduce no shipping change. The [direction contract](./audits/unified-player-deck-direction-2026-10-18.md) and [review record](./audits/unified-player-deck-2026-10-18.md) retain the exact evidence and limits. This is local completion only; no deployment, live-provider certification or physical-device certification is included.

## Pop-under restoration — 2026-10-17

Restored the existing publisher-supplied pop-under integration on public browse routes after finding the mount/component had disappeared from the active checkout while older ignored release payloads still contained its exact tag. The player, Watch, Settings, Admin, sign-in, sign-up and onboarding remain excluded. The device-local rolling limit is four script injections per hour with at least 30 minutes between claims; Web Locks serialize concurrent claims across tabs where supported. The Ad status choice remains fail-closed until preferences resolve and opting out reloads only a browsing page that already loaded the script. Display banner slots remain unmounted. Privacy, CSP, product, architecture and component guidance now describe the active behavior.

Web-only image `consumit-web:popunder-20261017-v1` is live and healthy; the prior anime-provider web image is retained and no API, database, or provider configuration changed. Production `/movies` and `/privacy` return 200, the response CSP admits the exact publisher script host, and the deployed static bundle contains the owner-supplied tag. A headless production browse check mounted the tag and recorded one injection while intercepting the publisher request before execution, so it verified integration without opening an ad window. Local checks passed 23 focused web tests, web type checking, targeted lint, and the optimized production Docker build. Real-network ad fill and popup delivery were not triggered by the smoke check.

## Pop-under cadence update — 2026-10-17

Changed the device-local cap to at most four publisher-script injections in any rolling 30-minute window, with at least five minutes between them. The client prunes timestamps outside that window before checking the cap; Web Locks continue to serialize claims across tabs. Settings and Privacy now show the same cadence. Focused web coverage verifies the rolling-window cap, five-minute gap, and timestamp pruning. Production rollout is web-only and retains the prior pop-under v1 image for rollback.

Deployed as `consumit-web:popunder-20261017-v2` (`sha256:be874f8ec05c4dce9fe8435a34c7afe0633396f366b91f5b143f53b46d976c7a`); the production web container is healthy, and the other production containers were not recreated. `/movies` and `/privacy` return 200, and the CSP still allows the exact publisher host. The live headless check confirms a recent injection blocks another until five minutes, four in-window injections hit the cap, and a fresh device claims one injection. The ad request was intercepted before execution; popup display/fill from the third party was not measured. The local Docker web is healthy. The 23 focused web tests, typecheck, targeted lint and optimized local and production Docker builds passed.

## Anime resolver recovery — deployed 2026-10-17

Production API and playback hotfixes address three shared causes: the internal provider contract now accepts the 70 configured overrides (it previously capped them at 64 and returned 500), the deployed API now supplies canonical anime titles and aliases to the newly added adapters, and a small provider-scoped anime set uses the playback service's DNS-pinned transport when the Resolver Worker egress rejects or stalls. Provider source validation and exact configured host scopes are unchanged. The resolver Worker and default/Europe media-relay policies also include the reviewed title-routing and exact anime CDN-host updates. The newest YumeZone patch queries its signed upstream lanes sequentially, matching the provider's observed request pattern.

Production readiness returned `ok` after deployment. Live Admin inspection for Attack on Titan (AniList 16498, episode 1) now resolves AllAnime to three validated choices and resolves AnimeKai and MeguAnime. This does not mean every provider is restored: on that same sample Miruro was unavailable, KotoTV timed out, FireAnime and YumeZone returned empty, and ReCloud/Kyren/Zenbu/9Anime/AniWave were unavailable or empty. These outcomes can also vary by title, episode and audio. AllAnime/MeguAnime success and service health are lookup evidence, not decoded playback acceptance. The [anime provider gap audit](./audits/anime-provider-gap-2026-10-17.md) records the fixes and remaining provider-specific failures. Do not describe full anime-provider coverage as complete.

## Five-provider follow-up — local Docker, 2026-10-17

FireAnime's nested Connect-RPC payload caused its valid AniList mapping to be silently replaced with Zod defaults; it now unwraps `data`. KoToTV's watch page redirected from `player.kototv.to` to `kototv.to`, which the DNS-pinned transport correctly blocked until the canonical host was explicitly configured. FireAnime's VOE flow also needed its observed `voe.sx` and mirror resolution hosts, plus an exact pattern for the 18-character `ugc-cdn-caching-n*.cloudwindow-route.com` media family. Zenbu's production-origin Direct HLS requests fail in the browser, while its CDN hosts are scoped for Relay. MegaVid's Direct route passed a fresh production-origin first-frame sample; Anixo's did not and remains Relay.

The updated local Docker API is healthy. A workstation-side adapter diagnostic loaded the local roster and current catalog metadata, then tested browser delivery at the local Docker page origin. On One Piece (AniList 21, episode 1; FireAnime's configured episode 21), all five returned a validated source and Chrome decoded a first frame. A separate browser check using the production site as its origin decoded FireAnime, KoToTV and MegaVid Direct; Anixo and Zenbu Direct failed there. These checks use current adapter output from this workstation, not the live production API/session pipeline or sustained playback. The provider config/policy edits are prepared locally; production deployment and full-duration acceptance are not claimed.

The [canary audit](./audits/provider-regional-cloudflare-canary-2026-10-12.md) records the measured per-provider regional Resolver/Media Relay slice and remaining acceptance checks.

Miami Relay and Embed dispatch recovery (2026-10-12): API image `e84060152707-20261012T011431612Z` activates the owner's approved Miami-only API lookup exception; video remains on the Cloudflare Media Relay and the OVH media node stays off. Real deployed Watch checks for Fight Club and Pulp Fiction decode 720p, seek to 60 seconds and resume; all 39 observed Cloudflare media responses return 200. A VTT upload stays selected and renders. Resolver Worker `8aac1c9b-c929-4bbd-b610-973be827d766` removes server page probes from canonical movie/series frame dispatch while retaining exact host/identity validation and anime identity mapping. Vixsrc Embed plays in the browser; ApiPlayer/EmbedAPI provider verification and anime mapping failures remain unresolved. Explicit fresh provider choice now retries past automatic cooldown without bypassing concurrency or request budgets. API checks (112), targeted Embed/Worker checks, lint, package types, production build and HTTPS smoke pass. Existing disabled/removed settings are preserved. See the [recovery audit](./audits/cloudflare-media-relay-recovery-2026-10-12.md) and [updated ADR 0009](./adr/0009-cloudflare-provider-resolution.md).

Rigel resolution correction (2026-10-11): the member job is trying Rigel on the live site, where pinned production samples resolved but their manifests failed in the viewer. The adapter itself was separately broken on resolve: its verification walk read full dlproxy variant playlists through the shared bounded document reader, whose 1 MB cap a feature-length playlist routinely exceeds, so every candidate died with "Provider document exceeds its limit" and the resolve honestly returned empty — local movie and series probes both failed this way. Both walk legs now read a bounded 64 KB prefix (the segment probe was already bounded); the walk only needs the manifest head and its first reference, both of which sit at the top of any valid playlist. A new integration regression drives a greater-than-1 MB variant through the real document reader and requires the chain to verify. Live local re-probe: movie 550 and series 1396 S1E1 both resolve to four validated sources in about four seconds. dlproxy play manifests also answer 200 with ACAO `*` even when carrying the production Origin, so the resolve correction is the remaining browser-visible break; the change ships with the next API release. API checks (529, including the new regression), SDK checks (338), and API/SDK lint and type checks pass.


Cloudflare media-relay recovery check (2026-10-12): Media Relay Worker deployment `36bfdc49-0a84-49bf-b032-1dd85ca9186b` is live with the current bounded streaming implementation and 16 approved provider policies. Production-origin CORS preflight returns 204 and invalid grants are rejected with 403; 102 relay Worker tests pass. The production API `/health/ready` path is healthy. A live movie-550 WeCollege Miami Relay resolve returns `PLAYBACK_UNAVAILABLE`; a live LookMovie Worker resolve returns `network-error` at `lmscript.xyz`. The API allowlist has the same 16 routes and does not expose `lookmovie-relay`; OVH remains disabled. These failures occur before media delivery, so they do not establish a broken Worker stream. Keep LookMovie off the active Cloudflare route until its Worker-side lookup and full browser playback pass. See [the recovery audit](./audits/cloudflare-media-relay-recovery-2026-10-12.md).

Cloudflare provider resolver production connection (2026-10-11): Resolver Worker `e8f4fba7-22f2-4ab5-ad6a-aa2e3f226b74` is enabled and connected to API image `e84060152707-20261011T212711214Z`. A live movie-550 API request returned a Direct HLS session after Worker resolution and API source validation; sampled Worker logs show API-originated provider attempts. The 48-row Worker canary emitted candidates for 12 Direct, 6 Relay, and 6 Embed rows; Vixsrc and LookMovie remain unavailable from Cloudflare, and candidate output is not browser-playback acceptance. `PLAYBACK_NODE_ENABLED=false`; OVH media containers are stopped. The Cloudflare Media Relay remains enabled for its existing approved routes. See [ADR 0009](./adr/0009-cloudflare-provider-resolution.md), the [production audit](./audits/cloudflare-provider-resolver-production-2026-10-11.md), the [canary audit](./audits/cloudflare-provider-resolver-canary-2026-10-11.md), and the [routing runbook](./runbooks/cloudflare-provider-resolution.md).

Evion production seek diagnosis and watchdog correction (2026-10-11): the member job is watching Evion series on the live site, where Watch seeks time out. The Watch no-referrer policy was necessary but not sufficient. Live probes prove the whole Evion chain healthy from this network: the `api.bingr.one` resolve and every `img.animecurx.tech` playlist/segment answer 200/206 with ACAO `*`, unsigned stable URIs, dense per-segment IDR keyframes and standard H.264 High + AAC-ADTS; the real production page pinned to Evion decodes 1920×1080 and advances indefinitely from the start. The failure is specific to seeking: with the shipped player hls.js configuration, Evion seeks complete but their latency varies from 0.2 to 28.8 seconds, because the buffer only commits long after the target fragments download. Both playback cores treat any 8 seconds without `currentTime` movement during buffering as a fatal network stall; a live seek never moves `currentTime` until it commits, so every Evion seek longer than ~8 seconds is killed mid-flight — the deployed player destroys the engine around 14 seconds, matching the route-deployment audit's failed Watch seeks. Each core now grants an active seek its own 30-second budget (reset by `seeking`, released by `seeked`/`playing`/`pause`, still fatal for a truly dead seek); mid-playback stalls keep the 8-second budget and paused seeks stay exempt. The bounded-seek tests adopt the seek budget, and each core gains a slow-seek regression that must survive past the stall clock. All 232 UI checks, UI lint and UI type checks pass. These are local player changes; production seeks keep failing until the web image ships the correction.


Production Vixsrc Frame activation (2026-10-11): the member job is trying the measured working provider routes on the live site. API release `e84060152707-20261011T183838635Z` adds an exact-host, movie/series-only `vixsrcframe` Embed entry without an OVH document lookup; the original Vixsrc Direct entry remains separate. Existing 49 provider overrides are preserved. Real deployed Watch movie 550 and series 1396 S1E1 decode 1280×720 and advance for more than five seconds through the shared external-player surface. Provider controls and unavailable progress sync remain explicit; the reviewed plain-frame policy permits provider popups/top navigation. SDK checks (332), API registration checks (25), package types/lint, production build and HTTPS smoke pass. LookMovie's same-OVH relay passes another Watch seek, advancement and fresh-capability renewal sample; Purstream movie/series playback and SRT/VTT uploads pass again. Current provider sample results and rollback are recorded in the [deployment audit](./audits/provider-route-deployment-2026-10-11.md).

No-extra-subscription provider research (2026-10-11): operator screenshots identify the existing Canadian VPS-3 2027; OVH's matching offer includes unlimited traffic and 2 Gbps public bandwidth. This strengthens the existing bounded same-OVH fallback instead of buying another server, while actual sustained capacity still requires measurement. Fresh production-origin Chrome Fetch tests fail readable CORS at both Vixsrc and LookMovie lookup endpoints with no-referrer and legitimate origin-referrer policies; no-cors responses remain opaque. Vixsrc's published plain iframe independently decodes movie 550 and series 1396 S1E1 at 720p, starts at 60 seconds and advances. The standard sandbox control does not play. No accepted progress events were observed, so the plain frame is a measured provider-player candidate with an explicit frame-policy tradeoff, not native-player parity or a deployed route. [The consolidated research](./audits/no-extra-cost-provider-plan-2026-10-11.md) compares current/free compute, Workers limits, provider integration and the next bounded slices. This research changes no VPS setting, cloud account, production provider row or frame policy.

Aphrodite recipe refresh from PROVE (2026-10-11): the member job is still resolving Aphrodite titles instead of receiving atlantic.st's static 64-second decoy clip. The adapter now follows PROVE's current order — Helios first, because its master answers ACAO * and its rungs still carry full titles, with the current hl_/ms_/ns_ lane keys from the 2026-10-06 bundle; the signed cdn.hls.lol lane is a fallback that ships only when its first rung reaches ten minutes, matching PROVE's own `>= 600` gate, while a non-playlist SPA answer and a short rung both stay honest misses. The vendored signing chunk already matched PROVE's current `aphrodite-gate.mjs` byte for byte, and the Atlantic Referer hint still covers both lanes. Six SDK checks cover the new order, the decoy rejection, and the rotated lane keys alongside the signed-backend, 401-retry, and non-playlist cases; 330 SDK checks with SDK lint and type checks pass, and the twenty-three configured-provider checks still pass against the example roster. Live probing with the shipped first lane key resolves movie 550 through Helios to a full 8,348-second 1080p rung: the master sits on `stream.hls.lol`, its variant on `iad-01.transcode.cfd`, and its init segment and 1,392 segments on `totallyacdn.org`. The exact `stream.hls.lol` master host therefore joins the Aphrodite row's `mediaHosts` in the example roster and the local roster — it was already a resolution host, but `validatedSources` drops any source outside `mediaHosts`, so the lane could never be admitted before. The same probe records the lane's live referer matrix: variants answer 200 only for an exact-match Referer on `https://atlantic.st` or `http://localhost` and echo that origin as ACAO (the localhost echo drops the port), while no Referer, `https://consumit.online` and `https://www.movy.sx` all answer 403, and an allowed Origin alone never passes. Watch sends no Referer at all through its no-referrer policy, and the production origin is refused, so this Helios lane plays behind an Atlantic relay and not browser-direct from Consumit's Watch: the adapter header and the source-header comment now say so instead of claiming browser-direct, Aphrodite resolves honestly and the waterfall moves to the next provider rather than shipping the decoy, and this slice makes no production or browser-playback claim for the lane. The row stays Direct at the member's direction rather than becoming a relay route. Production enablement is prepared and operator-pended: the private transfer payload at `local/production-playback/aphrodite-update/` rebuilds the captured OVH state with exactly two changes — the aphrodite row gains the `stream.hls.lol` master host, and its production tombstone becomes the local `{ "priority": 100 }` override, which places Aphrodite last in both the movie and series attempt orders so it is reached only after the providers that already play — and it passes the settings-transfer validator with all 48 rows and 48 overrides. The remaining production steps are an API release carrying this adapter, the mounted providers file rewrite, and the private migration-connection sync.


Bounded LookMovie media fallback deployed (2026-10-11): release `e84060152707-20261011T170313613Z` adds the explicit `lookmovie-relay` route through a separate OVH media process and DNS-only `media.consumit.online`, keeping successful Direct routes and the existing Worker routes. LookMovie lookup, graph validation, media and renewal use the resolving OVH egress. Independent signed capabilities, public-DNS pinning, isolated media configuration, cancellation, request deadlines, aggregate pacing and a persistent daily quota bound the new route. All 48 pre-existing provider overrides remain identical; only the new relay override is added. The site/API keep their existing Tunnel. No server purchase or paid-plan activation occurred.

Public Chrome checks decode and seek two movies at both 1080p and 720p. Actual deployed Watch checks cover first frame, seeking, quality switching, provider captions, selected/rendered SRT and VTT uploads, and two concurrent viewers advancing for 160 seconds. A same-session API renewal returns a fresh capability that decodes in Chrome; automatic gapless expiry recovery, full-title playback and larger concurrency remain unverified. Purstream movie/series Direct playback and uploads pass again after this API rollout. SDK/API/media checks, types, lint, production build and HTTPS smoke pass. [Accepted ADR 0008](./adr/0008-provider-egress-and-media-routing.md), the [media runbook](./runbooks/ovh-media-relay.md) and the [deployment audit](./audits/ovh-media-relay-2026-10-11.md) record limits, rollback and remaining Vixsrc lookup-location work. The earlier private prototype and [official-source research](./audits/provider-delivery-architecture-research-2026-10-11.md) underpin this slice; other providers still require their own measured route.

Production playback corrections deployed (2026-10-11): release `e84060152707-20261011T021152629Z` ships the Watch referrer policy, Purstream's adapter-owned raw-VTT subtitle-playlist handling, and both playback cores' uploaded-subtitle selection correction. Twelve guarded runtime source files were installed after exact revision/source checks. The production build and HTTPS smoke pass; saved provider base and all override settings retain matching hashes. Prior images, source and environment remain available for rollback. No database migration or paid-plan activation occurred.

Seven real Chrome Watch samples on `https://consumit.online` decode, seek and resume: Purstream movie/series, Evion series, CookieBakers movie/series, AnimeParadise and Kazora. The harness only pins the provider on the real resolve request; deployed scripts, headers, session responses and media are untouched. Purstream exposes 1,805/587 native subtitle cues, and uploaded SRT/VTT files both stay checked and render captions in the deployed player. UI (230), SDK (326), API (510) and contracts (24) checks passed, with additional route/contract regressions passing separately. Changed runtime lint and package type checks pass; the production web build performs its type check. Eight fixture Chrome scenarios cover referrer navigation/Embed isolation, raw-VTT playback and uploads. These are sampled Chrome checks, not full-title or physical Safari certification.

The remaining Direct incompatibilities are external and are not marked fixed: Vixsrc challenges OVH IPv4/IPv6 and the tested lookup Worker; LookMovie's exact playlists and first/middle video segments work from their issuing OVH egress but return eleven non-media bytes on Windows. Viewer-IP hints are ignored. AnimeKai likewise serves OVH while the same graph returns 404 on Windows. A temporary lookup-only Worker resolved Recloud and Vidrift with the current adapters, but its issued links still failed browser delivery; the probe is removed. Existing Direct → approved Relay → Embed behavior remains active. The [production parity audit](./audits/provider-production-parity-2026-10-10.md) records the sanitized evidence and remaining delivery-path work.

Production Direct diagnosis and Watch referrer correction (2026-10-11): the exact same LookMovie links work only from their issuing egress in the operator's paired Windows/OVH test; production Origin makes no difference. A resolver-only Worker therefore cannot fix those samples. Vixsrc separately receives a server-side 403 challenge on OVH. Evion accepts the production Origin but rejects the Watch Referer; Watch now omits Referer through its response header and route metadata, including client navigation. Leaving Watch restores the ordinary root policy. Provider modes and the Direct → Relay → Embed order are retained.

Five deterministic Chrome checks pass for native/HLS decoded playback, initial/client navigation, policy restoration and the Provider Embed's explicit referrer policy; the original native test failed before the correction. Five live production-issued samples decode and seek under the diagnostic no-referrer policy: Evion series, CookieBakers movie/series, AnimeParadise and Kazora. Web type checking, targeted lint and the optimized build pass. These are local changes, not a production rollout or an all-provider guarantee. The [production parity audit](./audits/provider-production-parity-2026-10-10.md) records the egress comparison and remaining delivery failures.

Uploaded subtitle selection correction (2026-10-11): uploading SRT or VTT registered the track but the following track-list refresh reapplied saved language priorities, deselecting the upload because its language is unknown. Both playback cores now retain an explicit session track while it remains available. Unknown-language uploads enable captions without replacing saved language intent. Automatic language fallback still applies when a selected track disappears; intentional Off remains respected. The existing player design and upload validation are preserved.

All 224 shared UI tests, UI lint and UI type checks passed. The SRT/VTT upload regressions also passed with the next core selected. Two deterministic Chrome tests verify actual native caption rendering at zero sync offset, checked upload rows, file replacement, unchanged saved language order, invalid-file recovery and Off. Controller checks cover late discovery and source replacement. These checks use fixture media and local files; no production deployment is included.

OVH parity installation observed (2026-10-10): the operator supplied the completed rollout log for `e84060152707-20261010T233736045Z`; all 48 override records match the local snapshot, API health and HTTPS smoke pass. The actual OVH sweep resolved 28/47 samples, so provider readiness remains open. A production-API Chrome reproduction confirms Vixsrc returns 503 before media delivery, while LookMovie issues Direct links returning non-HLS content from Windows despite HTTP 200. Locally resolved candidates still decode and seek from the production origin, ruling out a blanket production-origin explanation for these two failures. A safe hop-level diagnostic passes on local Linux and awaits operator execution on OVH through the encrypted SSH key. The [parity audit](./audits/provider-production-parity-2026-10-10.md) records the updated evidence; earlier pending-installation text below is historical.

Local provider parity for OVH (2026-10-10, prepared; installation pending): the actual running local API's managed database supplies 48 base rows and 48 durable overrides, with 31 active rows after disabled controls and six tombstones. The private transfer preserves all per-kind ordering, roles, delivery modes and bounded operator controls; it replaces only provider settings transactionally with audit and one revision notification. Four validation tests and two disposable-database transaction/rollback tests pass, alongside 510 API tests, 326 SDK tests, API/SDK lint/types, API bundles and thirteen production environment tests. A 47-sample resolver sweep returned 42 validated results. Production-origin Chrome checks decoded and seeked sixteen of twenty-four Direct samples; Megavid, Anixo and Zoko also decoded and seeked independently through the production Worker. Kyren, Evion and Vidrift have observed production-origin restrictions, Purstream returns raw VTT where its master advertises subtitle playlists, and several title/provider samples remain unavailable or intermittent.
