See [the parity audit](./audits/provider-production-parity-2026-10-10.md) for source scope, exact active orders and evidence limits.

OVH production relay wiring (2026-10-10, in progress): the production file contains the local roster's 48 base rows with 47 enabled, but the running OVH API reports relay disabled, no origin or secret and zero approved relay providers. A public movie-550 request pinned to the locally enabled Rive Zephyr relay row returns `BAD_REQUEST` before resolution. A separate `consumit-media-relay-production` Worker is now deployed using the existing sixteen exact-host policies and three reviewed discovery recipes, with a distinct secret and `APP_ORIGIN=https://consumit.online`; production CORS preflight returns 204, rejects localhost with 403, and the incumbent local Worker still returns 204 for localhost. Transfer validation exposed the production wrapper's JSON-style environment quoting truncating relay maps under Node `parseEnv`; preparation and build now share literal dotenv serialization. The real round-trip regression failed before the fix and all thirteen production-config checks pass afterward; Node and Docker Compose interpret all five staged relay fields identically. Private ignored transfer files include guarded helper updates and environment backup handling. VPS installation, the original pinned request after installation, browser decoding/seeking and investigation of remaining direct-provider failures are pending; this records infrastructure progress rather than a completed playback launch gate.

Configured flux patterns reach the media destination gate (2026-10-10, at the member's request): the member job is watching KissKH titles instead of losing the provider to `empty` on every attempt. The row's reviewed flux patterns (`^hls[0-9]*\.cdnvideo11\.shop$` for its rotating masters, `^hls[0-9]+\.[a-z0-9-]+\.site$` for its segment family) were compiled for source validation but never reached the DNS-pinned transport's destination gate, so the adapter's master liveness check on `hls03.cdnvideo11.shop` threw `Blocked media destination.` and every KissKH probe resolved `empty`. Each configured row now compiles its patterns once and threads them through `createProviderHttp` into the transport, which revalidates every hop and redirect against exact hosts plus the row's patterns; separate-audio's own fetch builds with them too, while ADR 0005 discovery scopes stay exact-host-only. CookieBakers needed no code change: movie 550 and series 1396 resolve repeatedly on the real config, and its probe failures trace to upstream resolution-route windows lasting minutes (the 14:53 and 15:25 rounds both failed while 18:38 and 19:08 passed) — beyond any adapter retry spread, during which the waterfall correctly reports unavailable for that title and moves on; its stale header comment claiming Worker relay delivery now matches the audited browser-direct registration. Live evidence: KissKH and CookieBakers movie and series probes all return ok in the rebuilt container (kisskh 1772/1149 ms, cookiebakers 922/679 ms), and the real-config trace resolves Inception's master on `hls03.cdnvideo11.shop` with SRT subtitles. 506 API checks (three new: transport gate, provider-http forwarding, KissKH wiring), 326 SDK checks, and api/SDK lint and type checks pass; the api container was rebuilt with the playback and release overlays.

Rate-limited pop-under advertising and support card (2026-10-10, at the member's request): browse surfaces now load the owner's pop-under network script at most twice per rolling hour per device, tracked in Consumit's own local storage (`consumit.ads.popunders`, pruned hourly, malformed store counts as empty, document-level guard prevents double injection). `FilScreenShell` mounts the component everywhere except `/settings` and `/admin`; Watch, authentication, and onboarding use reduced shells and never receive a pop-under, so playback and private work stay free of them. A dismissible Home card (`shared/AdSupportCard`) explains that advertisements pay for Consumit's servers, bandwidth, and catalog data, keeping the app free; dismissal is remembered per device (`consumit.ads.support-card-dismissed`). The Privacy Policy discloses the second advertising partner, its third-party cookies, and the two-per-hour cap. This supersedes the earlier banner-only preference recorded in the 2026-10-04/2026-10-08 entries and the placement audit's no-popunder recommendation. Six popunder rate-limit checks and three support-card checks pass alongside the full 468-test web suite with lint and type checks. Live-network popup behavior itself is not unit-testable and was not observed in this slice; the injection budget is the enforced bound. Containers were not rebuilt.

Role-opted separate audio (2026-10-09): the member job is selecting an available language — Vixsrc's Italian and Purstream's French/English included — while any video server plays. Separate-audio discovery is now gated by the operator's provider role, not an adapter-declared capability: every enabled Audio source or Both row approved for the member's region contributes tracks, with dedicated Audio source rows sorting first, and a provider whose adapter never declares a label still discovers (its id labels the tracks). This supersedes the reviewed-adapter-capability gate recorded in the 2026-10-03 entries below; [ADR 0004](./adr/0004-opt-in-separate-audio.md) records the owner decision. One budget-exhausted row no longer hides the other opted-in rows' tracks — the busy error is returned only when no row could run at all. Live probing of a Vixsrc movie master confirms separate Italian and English audio renditions on the allowlisted `vixsrc.to` host, and live Purstream movie and series masters confirm French and English renditions on the allowlisted `free.finepulfe.xyz` host with valid media playlists behind them; Purstream's local and example rows move from Video source to Both, and the Purstream adapter now matches series rows upstream labels `tv` (it already matched `show`), fixing series resolution for that provider. With those rows on Both the extra tracks are discoverable from any current server. 500 API tests (including four separate-audio tests covering the no-label Both row, budget-skipped rows, and the all-busy error), the provider-management web tests, and 272 SDK tests (including Purstream's `show`/`tv` series fixtures) pass; API/SDK type checking and targeted lint pass, and pre-existing web type errors in unrelated in-flight experience/profile files remain out of scope. Roles still do not verify compatibility, and provider-wide audio health stays untested.

Single frontend (2026-10-09): Consumit retains `apps/web` and `packages/ui` as its only frontend and shared interface package. The duplicate `apps/next-web`, `packages/next-ui`, frontend-specific commands, and Docker overlay are removed. Existing member flows and the Consumit Player remain in the active frontend.

VidZen source recovery (2026-10-08): the member job is watching a VidZen title in Watch instead of losing the provider to `Unauthorized: Missing authentication token`. Since 2026-10-08 the source API mints nothing and refuses every request without the per-title `apiToken` the site's own player page carries, so the adapter now reads `/movie/{id}` or `/tv/{id}/{s}/{e}` first, takes the first signed `eyJ…` token anchored on `apiToken`, and passes it through: a page that withholds the token fails as `unavailable` after a single request, a 404 page stays `empty`, and a token for another title comes back 401 Token ID mismatch. The same check found `/api/stream/…` answering a missing referrer with 401 and any foreign referrer or origin with 403, so every source now carries the `vidzen.fun` Referer hint the server-side verification fetch needs. Delivery stays browser-direct on purpose: each media playlist re-rolls the third-party segment hosts it names on every fetch, so no exact-host relay lease can ever cover the graph — a relay row resolves but never plays — while the segments themselves answer 200 with `access-control-allow-origin: *`. Live Watch on the local stack plays it end to end: series 235930 S1E1 and movie 27205 both land `delivery: direct` under the `?provider=vidzen` pin, the gated master and variant answer 200, every rotating segment host answers 200, and the player reaches readyState 4 with time advancing and no page errors. No embed path answers on the site itself, movie 550's own stream still answers 502 upstream (that title's provider-side outage), and the production origin `https://consumit.online` is refused by VidZen's allowlist, so production playback falls back to the remaining providers until either VidZen allows that origin or an owner-approved relay capability learns playlist-discovered hosts. 271 SDK and 477 API checks pass with workspace lint and type checks; ARCHITECTURE, the readiness table, the relay runbook and the adapter header record the gate and the delivery choice.

PROVE fleet additions (2026-10-08): Viduki, Netoda, Purstream, Vidrift, StreamVault, and Fabric join the local roster from PROVE at the owner's request — three direct rows and three relay rows, all enabled, with probes on movie 550 and series 1396. Each adapter is ported behind the provider seam with hostile-input validation (a live-data null-field find converted the ports to nullish parsing with per-item fallbacks). StreamVault unwraps its proxy wrapper to direct upstream media only when no browser-unsendable headers are required; Fabric reuses the reviewed vidnest lane as its own row while the zeta tombstone is retained. Exact-host relay approvals for Viduki, Netoda, and Fabric are in both the API and Worker maps; the Admin attempt-order list hides disabled rows while saving the complete order. Verification: 269 SDK, 477 API, and full web checks with lint and type checks; admin probes resolve all six rows; guest Watch decodes purstream and streamvault on movie 550 with captures. Vidrift lanes are HEVC-only and do not decode in headless Chromium. Relay playback still needs `wrangler deploy --config infra/cloudflare/media-relay.local.jsonc` with operator credentials. See [the audit](./audits/prove-fleet-additions-2026-10-08.md) for evidence and limits.


Sandbox-refusing provider frames (2026-10-08): the member job is watching a MegaPlay episode in Watch instead of reading "Opss! Sandboxed our player is not allowed. Remove sandbox to use it." inside the frame. Frame sources now carry a declared frame sandbox policy from the frame adapter: MegaPlay and VsEmbed, whose published players react to the `sandbox` attribute itself — a permissive sandbox that also allows pop-ups and top navigation is still refused — resolve as `frameSandbox: 'none'` and render as plain iframes, while every other frame keeps the standard scripts/same-origin/forms/presentation sandbox with pop-ups and top navigation withheld. Live browser samples on the real hosts show the difference: the MegaPlay player reaches a ready video element on a 1,478-second MSE stream only without a sandbox, VsEmbed loads its actual player poster only without one, both refuse every sandboxed variant, and ReAnime still plays sandboxed. A live run against the local stack resolves MegaPlay, APIPlayer and VsEmbed into Watch with no refusal copy: the two refusing frames carry no `sandbox` attribute, APIPlayer keeps `allow-scripts allow-same-origin allow-forms allow-presentation`, and the browser still blocks those frames from touching the parent document. The policy stays frame-only: SDK validation rejects it on media sources, contracts and playback sessions carry it to Watch and the Admin preview, and the relay never grants it. Outside reload, Server selection, failure states, the isolated `noopener noreferrer` new-tab link and the "Progress sync is unavailable" copy are unchanged. 269 SDK, 20 contract, 477 API and 414 next-web checks pass, 415 of 416 web checks pass — the remaining web failure is the unrelated in-flight disabled-order edit — and workspace lint and type checks pass. ADR 0006 records the provider-scoped boundary weakening; PRODUCT, ARCHITECTURE and COMPONENTS record the surface.

OVH + AWS production release (2026-10-08): the operator job is launching the original frontend at consumit.online within a 250 MAD monthly reserve. The approved release isolates a fresh PostgreSQL 17 database with separate application/migration/backup roles and private-CA TLS, uses encrypted pgBackRest full/WAL and Restic logical/config backups in versioned, Governance-locked S3, and sends authentication through SES with bounded sends and private feedback. Free Cloudflare rules, a closed-until-verified registration gate, Telegram alerts, independent outage monitoring, gross-usage budgets and credit-expiry checks are supplied with private setup/recovery tooling. Local tests and the disposable TLS/logical/PITR/backlog drill pass; live accounts, real offsite restores, actual Telegram/SES/HTTPS browser checks and the public fifty-session baseline remain explicit gates because the VPS has not been purchased. See [the deployment runbook](./runbooks/ovh-aws-production.md) and [ADR 0007](./adr/0007-ovh-aws-production.md).

Permanent icon rail and guest portrait (2026-10-08): the desktop sidebar no longer expands — it stays a slim icon rail whose brand mark links home, with the wordmark and show/hide control serving the mobile strip alone; any open state falls back to the rail at desktop widths. Guests share one fixed generated portrait instead of a random letter, shown in the header and on the guest profile screen with no stored state. Shell, home, and state tests cover the rail lock, the home links, and the portrait; lint and type checks pass. Deployed desktop and mobile captures confirm the rail, the home navigation, and the mobile strip with no page errors. DESIGN records the permanent rail.

First frontend release preparation (2026-10-08): the operator job is producing a reproducible Consumit release at `https://consumit.online` while preserving the working local app. Readiness fixtures isolate developer settings, production preparation generates private secrets and exact HTTPS origins, and the production wrapper selects only its private environment with playback/monitoring included. Example peppers, weak backup URI TLS and incomplete service configuration fail before launch. Cloudflare authentication rules target the mounted `/v1/auth/` routes and private APIs bypass edge caching. The standard root test command passes 1,985 checks; thirty disposable integration checks, all workspace lint/type checks, gateway configuration and Terraform validation pass. Required Docker manifests and support files belong to the release snapshot. VPS provisioning, real service credentials, offsite restore, live alerts/email and actual HTTPS playback remain gates; see [the readiness audit](./audits/deployment-readiness-2026-10-08.md).

Player and rail refinements (2026-10-08): the sidebar toggle and the mobile show-sidebar control now carry the Consumit mark instead of the panel icons, keeping the Hide/Show sidebar accessible names. Trending Movies, Series, and Anime rails moved the flame beside the heading text, leaving View All as a plain link. The player transport no longer has a standalone HD quality trigger; quality stays one step inside the Settings panel. The pause overlay headline shows the bare series/anime name at one fixed size instead of appending the episode title at a responsive size. UI tests cover the settings-path quality flow, the pause headline, and the heading icon; web rail, watch, shell, and full suites pass with lint and type checks. Deployed captures confirm the flame placement, the HD-free transport, and the guest mark with no page errors.

Guest profile experience (2026-10-08): every signed-out visitor now shares one stable "G" guest character with no per-tab random letter and no session storage. Opening the avatar renders a guest profile — shared character portrait, "Browsing as a guest." headline, and Sign in / Create account actions that retain `/library?section=profile` as the destination — instead of a bare sign-in redirect. Other library sections keep the existing sign-in redirect. Covered by updated shell, routing, and legal tests; lint and type checks pass. Desktop and mobile captures were inspected; the deployed container serves the new screen with no page errors. PRODUCT, COMPONENTS, DESIGN, and the Privacy page record the new flow.

Live Adsterra display advertising (2026-10-08): the four owner-supplied banner units (728×90, 468×60, 320×50, 300×250) now serve through the shared AdSlot on Home, Movies/Series catalogs, title details, and the owner's Profile overview. Each near-viewport slot measures its own container, loads only the fitting unit inside an isolated frame with an independent snippet global, reserves exact dimensions under a quiet Advertisement label, stays invisible until the creative lands, and collapses when the creative is blocked or unfilled; hidden and too-narrow slots never load. Series/Anime Watch keeps its layout-only preview and playback never depends on an ad decision. The Privacy Policy discloses the partner's third-party cookies. Eight slot checks plus legal, screen, lint, and type checks pass. Deployed desktop/mobile captures show correct units with live ad-host requests and no page errors; zones without fill collapse cleanly.

Legal documents (2026-10-07): Terms of Service, Privacy Policy, and DMCA Notice pages carry a centered Charter display title, a muted Last updated stamp, and numbered Charter sections with an orange left rule on the crucial notice and orange report-form links. The shell footer links all three from every page. Five legal checks, web lint, and type checks pass; desktop and mobile captures were inspected and the deployed routes serve 200.

History import removal (2026-10-07): the Letterboxd history import is removed end to end — web import screens, API routes and worker, contracts schemas and error codes, the compose service, and four npm dependencies — while staging tables and provenance columns are retained. API, contracts, and UI checks pass with lint and type checks; the old web and API import routes return 404 on the deployed stack and the orphan worker container is removed.


Anime and episode Watch live advertisements (2026-10-08): the member job is browsing anime and opening episodes with the same advertisement treatment as Series. Anime details compose the existing live AdSlot below the hero, plus a wide-layout Overview rectangle or a narrow-layout banner before Overview. Series and Anime Watch replace their preview with one live banner above the full player and episode-rail row. Ad loading remains independent of playback and inherits exact fitting dimensions, isolated snippets, and blocked/unfilled handling. Sixty-one existing anime-detail, ad-slot, episode-rail and playback checks, changed-file lint, and the optimized Docker build with TypeScript checks pass. Actual localhost integration confirms filled anime-detail and Watch banners, configured live scripts on both episode Watch routes, 728×90 desktop and 320×50 mobile units, no preview markup, no ad inside the player, and no page overflow. Desktop/mobile anime captures cover both themes; Watch retains its existing dark presentation. The rebuilt local web reports healthy; other services and database volumes are preserved, with the previous web image retained for rollback.

Settings and featured rotation refinement (2026-10-08): the member jobs are choosing readable subtitle priorities, finding the current Settings section while scrolling, and browsing featured titles without an unintended pause. Preferred languages now use an ordered, device-persisted list with up to three choices, shared Add/Move/Remove controls, explicit 44px targets, empty/full states, and focus restoration after controls disappear or become disabled. The rail tracks the reading section and Account returns to its in-page section. Featured rotation distinguishes keyboard focus from retained mouse focus and touch selection, retains intentional hover/offscreen/hidden-tab/reduced-motion pauses, and keeps a visible slide when candidates shrink. Twenty focused behavior tests, changed-file lint, and the optimized Docker build with TypeScript checks pass. Final deterministic Settings captures cover desktop/mobile and both themes; live browser checks verify navigation, focus recovery and no mobile overflow. The main Home hero advances after mouse selection while focus remains on the selector. The local web was rebuilt and restarted and reports healthy; other services and database volumes are preserved, with the prior web image retained for rollback.

Advertisement sizing correction (2026-10-08): the member job is browsing with fully visible advertisements that need no internal scrolling. Player recovery/control refinement (2026-10-04): the initial unavailable panel now offers lazy Server discovery before a session exists, and resolve honors an explicit eligible configured server while retaining canonical identity and the saved native checkpoint. Requested Direct/Relay/Embed additions (2026-10-04): ten owner-selected providers are installed and enabled locally. RiveZephyr Watch playback (2026-10-04): Admin's ninety-second preview played while Watch's exact thirty-minute grant received relay 403s from the maximum-lifetime validator. Player labels and stable controls (2026-10-03): the member job is selecting readable subtitles without the playback clock jumping or a persistent volume slider taking space. Validation passed 187 UI tests, 117 subtitle API tests, UI/API type checking, relevant lint and eight deterministic desktop/touch browser checks. Optional server selection (2026-10-03): the member job is choosing another configured playback source when the current one is slow or unsuitable. Loading, list failure/retry, no alternatives and busy selection use the existing panel. Shared panel headers and settings rows retain their layout on mobile. Product and glossary wording now permit this optional choice.

Validation passed 186 shared UI tests and 53 Watch/coordinator tests, UI/web lint and type checking, and four deterministic desktop/touch Chrome browser scenarios. The final optimized web image built successfully and replaced the local web container. API and web report healthy; About and Watch movie 550 return 200. Served Watch assets contain the Auto selector, discovery-failure recovery copy and mobile panel fix.

Continuous proxy movie slice (2026-10-03): the member job is watching a whole movie without a visible refresh at each healthy lease boundary. Watch-page MSE HLS renews access in the background while retaining its engine and buffer; original fragment addresses map to fresh capabilities only after matching VOD timeline/rendition metadata and AES-128 keys. A regression fails on expired segment requests before the fix and crosses two renewals with one engine afterward. The actual optimized Chromium player on movie 522406 crosses a forced ninety-second Worker expiry with zero reloads, pauses, stalls, fallbacks or failed media requests; the expired old capability separately returns 403. This checks the expiry mechanism, not a full-title endurance guarantee. Native/cast/live and real upstream failures retain existing recovery. The [boundary audit](./audits/proxy-gapless-2026-10-03.md) records evidence. Workers Free remains active.

Proxy renewal correction (2026-10-03): the member reports movie 522406 repeatedly refreshing at about five minutes. Live Zephyr, Boron and Miami sessions each expose 290–295-second deadlines; aggregate metrics include successful expired-reason relay renewals. A regression reproduces the five-minute synthetic capability cutoff. Known upstream deadlines and direct behavior remain unchanged. The API correction is live; old grants require fresh resolution/renewal. See [the renewal audit](./audits/proxy-renewal-522406-2026-10-03.md) for checks and continuous playback evidence.

Cross-title relay correction (2026-10-03): movie 1032863 reproduced the same policy problem after the earlier 1101383 patch. Boron and both WeCollege city adapters now explicitly validate and lease bounded exact public destinations per session, with independent API/Worker recipe opt-ins. Their HLS playlists compile in the API control plane to avoid bulk signing under Free Worker CPU limits; all segment/key/map bytes remain at the Worker. Sniff-verified TS assets retain byte checks and now stream natively after prefix restoration. Actual Player decoding and seeking pass for Boron/Miami on 1032863, 1101383, 550 and 1022789; Miami also passes 1084242, whose Boron upstream returns 403. A final Boron 1022789 seek/soak passes beyond two minutes with 7,810 frames. Regression suites, focused type checks/lint and builds pass. Free remains active, Boise's disabled override is preserved, and independent BingeBang/Zeta/Nebula failures remain open. See [the cross-title audit](./audits/relay-cross-title-2026-10-03.md); the earlier per-title policy additions below are historical.

Miami fixed-quality selection (2026-10-03): one server exposes validated same-city resolutions through Consumit Player's existing Quality menu. Previous-proxy regression correction (2026-10-03): the owner identifies the earlier Consumit Cloudflare Worker as the working reference. The replacement relay's HLS assets now use native workerd piping rather than allocating a JavaScript promise/timer per chunk, with bounded transfer deadlines, abort propagation and enforced known lengths. Zephyr movie 550 failed after seeking in the prior path and passed a repeated check plus a three-minute 4K soak with native piping. Thirty Worker tests, type checking, lint and build pass. This addresses the sampled early delivery stop; separate edge root failures and intermittent resolution remain open. The Worker stays Free; no server migration is activated. See [the regression audit](./audits/previous-proxy-streaming-2026-10-03.md).

Movie 550 differential comparison (2026-10-03): all six previously failing movie relay providers decode and seek in the actual Consumit Player when the identical current relay executes locally in an isolated browser diagnostic. Movie/TV relay correction (2026-10-03): installed providers tested from Scout now use their saved delivery mode. Exact TV hosts are approved for Storm Boron and both WeCollege cities. Verified TS segments from reviewed Storm/Zeta hosts can stream despite misleading HTML content types. Compact encrypted grants reduce repeated encoding work that exceeded the Free Worker CPU limit. Admin Direct / Proxy selection (2026-10-03): Video delivery is a staged per-provider setting, persisted and audited with the existing role/limit controls. Save hot-applies the declared pool, including cached resolution, pending attempts and same-provider switching. Configured diagnostic previews use the chosen path; Scout now follows the saved path for installed rows, and added audio stays direct. The user explicitly corrected the separate provider pools in the parallel playback-cost chat; this control follows that decision rather than adding a Both delivery mode.

70 focused API tests, 12 Admin UI tests and two local Postgres integration tests pass, with changed-file lint. 23 focused API tests, 10 Admin UI tests, changed-file lint and the optimized web build with web type checking pass. Desktop Chromium verifies 1024/1440/1920 layouts, preserved drafts/results, selected-only tests, decoding/seeking, export privacy, preview teardown, retry evidence reset and partial failure. Live API/store and metrics are available; none of three sampled additional candidates is claimed reliable or enabled. Broad API type checking retains existing retired-social/old-fixture errors. Independent exact-host policies, redirect/playlist validation, bounded manifests, streaming/ranges, kill switch and conservative delivery-tagged viewing telemetry support the trial; reusable defaults stay disabled and the local deployment uses Workers Free with an empty relay policy. Existing providers remain direct-only, and the owner will add distinct relay providers later. Provider iframe fallback, DASH relay, added-audio relay and P2P remain deferred. Activation and seven-day measurements are in the [trial runbook](./runbooks/worker-relay-trial.md). The owner selected Free for now; no paid subscription is activated. The live trial targets the current localhost:8080 browser origin.

Live Workers Free delivery passes controlled native ranges, HLS master/variant rewriting and a ranged segment. The earlier Vixsrc edge test applied an incorrect classification; Vixsrc is direct-only and that edge result is irrelevant to its direct compatibility. ## Active personal scope

The current member job is managing a private watching record and personal collections. Friends/Circle, Network, follower/following displays and archives, follow actions, public visibility controls, shared-list discovery, review likes, and popularity controls are retired. Older social implementation entries below are historical and do not define active scope. Removing these surfaces preserves stored member data. Delivery includes replacing the compiled web image and verifying the retired routes and navigation against that image.

Consumit is built in small, reviewable increments: foundations, primitives, component details, composed sections, static screens, then behavior and platform logic. Each increment leaves a visible and verifiable result without prematurely creating the full target workspace.

The unit of progress is the **smallest coherent slice**, not the smallest possible file. A slice includes the states and accessibility needed to judge it in context.

## Stage 0 — foundation

Completed for the initial workspace.

- Agree on product language and scope.
- Index active design references and deprecated concepts.
- Select Bun workspaces, Next.js web, Bun/Fastify API, and the shared UI boundary.
- Pin supported runtime and toolchain lines.
- Keep data stores, integrations, and speculative packages out until a slice demands them.

Exit criterion: the workspace installs, lints, type-checks, tests, and builds from one root command.

## Stage 1 — visual foundations

Current stage, paired with the first Stage 2 primitive.

Implement only the foundations exercised by the first slice:

- Color, type, spacing, radius, border, and motion tokens
- Page canvas and content container
- Focus, hover, disabled, loading, empty, and error conventions
- Icons and image treatment
- Responsive breakpoints derived from an actual composition

Exit criterion: tokens and base styles reproduce the selected reference's visual grammar without screen-specific values leaking into primitives.

## Stage 2 — primitives and small modules

Suggested first groups:

1. Text, icon, button, link, input, label, badge, divider, avatar, and progress primitives.
2. Poster/image frame, rating display, Taste Match signal, friend presence, and source-health indicator.
3. Form field, segmented control, filter chip, navigation item, tab, and compact action group.

For every module, define only the variants present in an active screen. Include keyboard behavior, focus visibility, text overflow, missing art, loading, disabled, and error states where relevant.

Exit criterion: modules can be reviewed in isolation and are already demanded by a named composed section.

## Stage 3 — composed sections

Compose primitives into deep, reusable product modules:

- Global navigation
- Content and continue cards
- Editorial feature
- Activity item/feed card
- Profile masthead
- Title masthead
- Episode row
- Search result and filter rail
- List row and curator panel
- Authentication form
- Watch header, Consumit Player, source selector, and playback report

Build only sections required by the next screen. Keep sample data at the section seam so visual work does not depend on production systems.

Exit criterion: each section handles its meaningful visual states and can be placed into a screen without duplicating its rules.

## Stage 4 — static screens

Assemble screens with deterministic local fixtures before wiring application logic. A practical order that maximizes reuse is:

1. Sign In and Create Account
2. Home / Discover
3. Title Detail and Series Detail
4. Watch
5. Help Center and Report Issue
6. Search / Explore
7. Public Profile and Profile Owner View
8. My Library
9. Friends Activity
10. List Detail
11. Settings
12. Three-step onboarding

This order is a proposal. Select the first screen after the technical foundation and first product slice are confirmed.

For each screen, verify the reference viewport, a narrow desktop/tablet width, keyboard navigation, long copy, empty/loading/error states, and responsive intent. Mobile receives a separate composition decision. Each screen also ships its SEO contract: route metadata (title, description, canonical), Open Graph/Twitter cards, and noindex where member-specific. Title and Series detail pages are landing surfaces with TMDB-driven metadata, JSON-LD, and crawlable overviews; sitemap and robots land once public routes stabilize.

Exit criterion: the screen is visually reviewable end to end with local fixtures and no production integration required.

## Stage 5 — product behavior by vertical slice

Add logic in user-valuable slices instead of wiring every backend at once:

The canonical catalog slice is implemented: real TMDB discovery, search, movie and series detail, seasons and episodes, normalized artwork, numeric routes, and explicit unavailable playback. Computed Taste Match, overlap, member scores, and numeric ratings stay hidden until their backing behavior is implemented. Every Watch action opens the dedicated first-party player route. The slice uses an injectable Fastify adapter, shared contracts, bounded upstream work, and deterministic network-independent tests.

Identity and the first durable member-data slice are implemented: verified email/password registration requires a chosen Username, opaque sessions live in Valkey, profiles and aliases live in Postgres, Settings performs real account mutations, and protected screens are server-authorized. Letterboxd ZIP/CSV imports are staged, matched through TMDB by a separate worker, reviewed, confirmed idempotently, and surfaced through real Profile, Library, and List screens. Unsupported social evidence renders zero or empty rather than fixtures. Member records cover the anime kind alongside TMDB movie/series ids — an additive `CatalogKind` migration stores AniList ids as `anime`, and progress, ratings, watched, favorites, diary, and title-review reads are proven across kinds whose numeric ids collide.

1. Identity: create account, verification, sign in, secure server session, recovery, profile, export, and deletion. **Implemented.**
2. Catalog: title identity, search, movie and series details. **Implemented for the TMDB metadata slice; `batch` and `search` now serve anime references through the AniList adapter inside the same versioned routes, and anime detail pages recommend titles from AniList instead of listing external site links.**
3. Library: native saves and Favorites plus imported watchlist, history, diary, ratings, reviews, and lists. **Durable import/read slice implemented; native diary creation, exact episodic diary identity, edit/delete, retry-safe writes, list/review edits, ratings, Top-4 favorites editing, profile/banner/avatar customization, and member-keyed playback progress with resume are implemented.** Playback completion creates a private, idempotent, exact-title-or-episode Watch History event in the same database transaction as progress. A member can deliberately copy a completed episode into Diary; Profile and Circle preserve and link its episode identity. Library pages Watch History separately from member-authored Diary, and account export includes both. Apply the diary, native/saved-list, watch-history, and diary-episode migrations before deploying the corresponding API.
4. Social: follow, activity, public reviews, and shared lists. **Member search, follow/unfollow, live follower counts, and a cursor-paginated Circle feed of public diary entries, reviews, and lists are implemented. Title pages show paged recent/popular/circle reviews from public profiles with author ratings and member review likes, plus a dedicated per-title reviews archive for movies, series, and anime. Members can create lists idempotently, edit title order and notes atomically, set visibility, delete owned lists, discover public lists from followed public profiles, and save/unsave another member's public list.**
5. Playback: resolve, session, source selection, reporting, fallback.
6. Personalization: **the Home Circle picks rail groups recent public watches/reviews by title and explains the followed-member evidence without a fabricated score.** Computed Taste Match, personalized ranking, and onboarding seeds remain unimplemented pending a reviewed signal model.

Each slice defines its interface, fixtures/fakes, persistence, errors, telemetry, and acceptance path. Introduce an adapter seam when a real second adapter exists or isolation is required.

Exit criterion: one complete member job works through the interface and its failure states, with appropriate automated checks.

Catalog-backed server pages use the versioned 24-reference batch contract through a shared deduplicating loader. A render enriches at most 120 unique title references and uses bounded parallel batches; large libraries and lists keep every member record and title route while rendering stable ID fallbacks for references outside that enrichment window. This prevents an imported history from turning one page request into unbounded catalog fan-out.

Public server-side TMDB and AniList metadata fetches use a five-minute Next Data Cache revalidation, matching the public catalog cache horizon while carrying no member credentials. Member HTML remains request-specific because the root layout reads the session; full route/CDN page caching is still deferred until that personalized shell is separated.

## Stage 6 — playback hardening

Playback starts with validated browser-direct HTTPS media, with an optional approved Worker fallback under ADR 0005. Admin saves one exact order per title kind; automatic recovery stays bounded and advances through the direct tier, then the approved relay tier. The API never relays video and member progress survives source changes. Public third-party embeds remain deferred.

The player-core rebuild is separately canaried with `NEXT_PUBLIC_PLAYBACK_CORE=next` on an edge-routed web deployment; `legacy` is the rollback value. Watch uses the same core in each deployment. Controlled browser tests must pass on the new path before canary traffic. Compare p95 first-frame time, playback-start success, buffering, fatal errors, and recovery success against the legacy deployment; the cutover requires at least 20% better controlled-fixture p95 first-frame time and no reliability regression. Keep the legacy build available until production comparison and Safari HLS verification are complete.

The provider operations slice stores overrides, revisions, and actor changes in Postgres. Apply the provider-operations migration before starting upgraded API replicas; the first replica imports the legacy override file under a database lock, then every replica polls the revision and applies effective settings. The opt-in playback Compose overlay starts an OpenTelemetry collector and Prometheus. Its alert rules and admin Operations tab show provider-failure conditions; connect an alert receiver and verify delivery before claiming unattended paging. Probe success means resolution succeeded, while viewer events establish playback health.

Hardening scope:

- Provider contract and contract tests
- Schema validation and SSRF controls
- Timeouts, concurrency limits, bounded provider waterfall, and circuit breakers
- Cache expiry, negative cache, single-flight, and stale refresh
- Playback sessions, progress, reporting, and source fallback
- Provider dashboard, health probes, and playback telemetry

Exit criterion: the configured test catalog meets the agreed playback success and latency baseline under normal and provider-failure scenarios.

### Stage 6 release closeout (2026-09-27)

Local checks pass: on 2026-09-28, `bun run check` completed workspace lint, typecheck, all 877 tests across 133 files, and optimized API/web builds. `bun run infra:validate` also passes both Compose configurations plus Terraform format, init, and validation with the pinned official `hashicorp/terraform:1.8.5` container fallback. The active Help Center and Report Issue routes use the reduced dual-theme shell, factual answers and recovery links, and a privacy-bounded report form. The user-provided public support inbox is configured in the local web and Compose environments; production must set the same `SUPPORT_EMAIL` value.

Validation follow-up (2026-09-28): a web jsdom screen test exceeded Vitest's five-second default under load. The web timeout is now 10 seconds; the web package rerun passed 255/255 tests, and the complete root `bun run check` then passed.

Watch History follow-up (2026-09-28): the completion-to-history slice passed contract, API, UI, and Watch checks. An isolated PostgreSQL 17 database was snapshotted before all 21 migrations and then passed 21 member-data/identity integration tests, including completion retries, rewatches, owner isolation, export, and tombstone deletion. Current package suites pass 883 tests across 133 files; workspace lint/typecheck stages and optimized API/web builds pass. The aggregate `bun run check` was interrupted by two stale playback fixture expectations, now corrected, and one load-sensitive five-second UI timeout; the affected case and full UI package passed on an isolated rerun.

Episodic Diary follow-up (2026-09-28): exact series/anime episode identity now flows from deliberate Watch History logging through the Diary API, Postgres, account export, public Profile, and Circle. The full root `bun run check` passed lint, typecheck, all 886 tests across 134 files, and optimized API/web builds. A disposable PostgreSQL 17 database was snapshotted to `consumit-diary-episode-pre-migration-20260928.dump` before applying all 22 migrations; 23 member-data and identity integration tests passed against it. No existing database was migrated. The additive `20260928030000_diary_episode_identity` migration still requires a named production snapshot before rollout.

The 2026-09-28 source audit and activation experiments are superseded by the current Admin activation controls and [2026-10-01 browser-direct audit](./audits/provider-direct-playback-2026-10-01.md). Current configured/enabled totals come from durable operator overrides; live browser evidence determines the retained roster.

Admin provider control supersedes the permission-review activation gate (2026-09-30): all configured rows, including disabled rows, enter the registry so Admin can enable or disable them. The loader no longer requires a `permissionReview` record; `/v1/admin/providers` reports live configured and enabled totals after durable overrides. Admin changes hot-apply across API replicas through the existing revision poll. The base configuration still supplies adapter identities, region/title-kind scope, and media-host allowlists; the resolver continues to validate sources and enforce delivery limits.

Web Vitals follow-up (2026-09-28): a small Next.js client component now reports CLS/FCP/INP/LCP/TTFB using the first-party `useReportWebVitals` hook. The browser sends only metric/value and one fixed page group, with credentials omitted; the strict API contract rejects extra fields and records bounded OpenTelemetry histograms without database storage. Prometheus p75 queries and bucket units are documented in `runbooks/web-vitals.md`. The complete root `bun run check` passed workspace lint, typecheck, all 908 tests across 139 files, and optimized API/web builds. Production p75 baselines remain open until representative deployed traffic arrives.

Public title ISR follow-up (2026-09-28): movie, series, and anime detail routes now render catalog-only HTML with a 300-second on-demand route cache. The root layout no longer reads member cookies; the client restores `/v1/me`, while title actions and reviews remain authenticated client reads. Search, discovery, and Watch catalog requests retain request-bound `no-store` behavior. The standalone production build returned `s-maxage=300` and `x-nextjs-cache: HIT` on the second request for each detail route. Web lint/typecheck passed and the six touched session/title-control test files passed 36/36. Production CDN propagation, catalog-change freshness, and deployed TTFB remain open for operator verification.

Player renewal follow-up (2026-09-28): Windows WebKit trace evidence showed source replacement resetting playback speed on both cores; the short fixture could then reach its end before a renewal assertion. The player now sends selected-speed changes directly to both controllers, which restore it at metadata, canplay, play completion, and delayed WebKit seek checks. Controller regression tests pass 24/24, full optimized Chrome playback suites pass 10/10 per core, and the focused repeated-lease/caption/paused-position scenario passes on both cores in Windows Chrome and WebKit. A final-code alternating Chrome benchmark produced 54 warm samples per core: p95 was 852 ms canary vs 888 ms legacy (4% faster), with all 114 warm starts reaching a frame. Paired p95s varied (1,325 / 680 / 877 ms canary; 1,531 / 930 / 811 ms legacy), so the 20% cutover gate is not met. Repeat on a stable runner; actual macOS/iOS Safari HLS remains unverified.

Library pagination follow-up (2026-09-28): replaced silent collection caps with bounded 30-row snapshots and stable owner-scoped keyset pages for saved titles, diary entries, owned lists, and saved lists. Per-title saved/watched state now uses the title-state read; save/watch mutations return 204 instead of re-sending the library. Library and Lists expose load-more/retry states, and saved-title search states that it covers currently loaded records. The last full `bun run check` passed lint, typecheck, 893 tests across 136 files, and optimized API/web builds. After that aggregate run, API route tests passed 22/22, the Library screen test file passed 8/8, and API typecheck passed. The API integration suite passed 36 tests across 7 files on isolated PostgreSQL 17 and Valkey containers; all 23 migrations, including `20260928040000_library_pagination_indexes`, applied after the named temporary dump `consumit-library-pagination-cursor-pre-migration-20260928.dump`. Existing databases were not touched. The index migration remains open for production rollout until operators take a named snapshot of the production database.

Personal-archive consolidation (2026-09-30): the standalone `/watchlist` and `/library` screens are retired. Saved titles stay in the owner-only profile Watchlist archive; completed Watch History now has an owner-only profile section with pagination, Watch again, and deliberate Log to diary. The sidebar and title activity links lead to profile sections. Old URLs redirect authenticated members to the corresponding profile sections, preserving bookmarks. Lists management remains at `/lists`; data endpoints and member records remain intact.

| Gate | State | Evidence needed to close |
|---|---|---|
| Player-core performance | **Open** | Both optimized cores pass the same 10/10 Chrome scenarios and the focused renewal scenario in Windows Chrome and WebKit. The latest pooled current-branch comparison before removing the redundant Watch session request used 54 warm samples per core: p95 854 ms canary vs 995 ms legacy (14% faster), p50 510 vs 559 ms. Batch p95s were 818 / 796 / 1,745 ms canary and 1,278 / 773 / 746 ms legacy; tails moved between pre-loadstart and media startup. A post-removal one-batch comparison is recorded in `PLAYER-REBUILD.md`; its tail and fixture telemetry resets make it inconclusive. The 20% cutover gate remains open. Remeasure alternating batches on a quiet runner and verify macOS/iOS Safari HLS before canary rollout. |
| Provider readiness | **Locally checked, production open** | The 2026-10-01 live browser audit covered all 62 configured rows on two sample sets and focused rechecks. Nine direct rows remain; each produced a Chrome first frame on a tested title. Lookmovie and CastleTV CDN allowlists were repaired. Catalog completeness, long-session reliability, region differences, and Safari remain unverified. See `audits/provider-direct-playback-2026-10-01.md`. |
| Direct-first media delivery | **Implemented; Free relay ready for future providers** | Direct providers run first, then exact-host-approved HLS/native media may use the edge Worker under ADR 0005. The API never carries video bytes. Provider compatibility, Free quota/CPU limits and seven-day measurements govern further rollout. |
| Alert delivery | **Open** | Configure a real receiver and verify a test alert reaches the operator; configured rules without a receiver do not provide paging. |
| Database migrations | **Open before rollout** | Take the named pre-migration snapshot, then apply `20260924010000_provider_operations`, `20260927010000_diary_idempotency`, `20260927020000_circle_activity_indexes`, `20260927030000_native_and_saved_lists`, `20260928010000_catalog_cache`, `20260928020000_watch_history`, `20260928030000_diary_episode_identity`, and `20260928040000_library_pagination_indexes` before upgraded API replicas. |
| Backups and restore | **Synthetic encrypted drill passed; production gate open** | A synthetic custom-format dump was sent to a loopback SFTP Restic repository, checked with 100% data reads, restored into a second disposable PostgreSQL 17 instance, and verified with migration status (23/23 up to date), one linked Member/Credential/Profile row, and a secret-free member export. The drill fixed fractional-second freshness parsing and host scoping, and exposed/fixed retention grouping by random temp path; backups now group by host/tag and default to 14 daily, 4 weekly, and 6 monthly snapshots. Repeating three same-day runs proved the intermediate snapshot is pruned. The running local sidecar reports successful local-volume dumps, which are not offsite or encrypted. Still verify a real Storage Box snapshot restore, seven-day provider PITR, separate-failure-domain retention, and production recovery duration before accepting members; measured local restore times are recorded in `postgres-recovery.md` and are not production RTO. |
| Infrastructure validation | **Closed locally** | `bun run infra:validate` passed both Compose profiles and `terraform fmt -check`, `terraform init -backend=false`, and `terraform validate` through the official `hashicorp/terraform:1.8.5` container fallback. No plan or apply was run. |
| Core Web Vitals baseline | **Instrumentation configured; baseline open** | Collect representative production traffic and inspect p75 LCP, INP, and CLS by bounded page group using [`web-vitals.md`](./runbooks/web-vitals.md). |
| Public title HTML cache | **Verified locally; deployed edge behavior open** | The optimized build and standalone server verify five-minute cache headers and repeat-request hits for movie, series, and anime title routes. Confirm the deployed CDN preserves this policy and measure representative title-page TTFB/LCP; member-specific endpoints must remain no-store. |
| Member support destination | **Configured locally** | `SUPPORT_EMAIL` is set to the user-provided public inbox in local web/Compose environments and tracked examples. Verify the deployed environment carries the same value; reports only open a member-reviewed email draft. |
| Canary and rollback | **Ready to exercise** | Keep the `legacy` player build and iframe JSON compatibility path available. Canary the session/core flags only after local test suites and the player gates pass; compare the documented startup/recovery metrics before widening. |

These are the remaining release criteria; they require controlled browser runs, deployment access, provider/Cloudflare review, or operator-owned recovery systems and cannot be inferred from a successful local build.

Full workspace verification follow-up (2026-09-28): after the session bootstrap, Watch session seed, duplicate Watch root-session-request suppression, and title-cache boundary changes, `bun run check` passed workspace lint, typecheck, all 912 tests across 140 files, and optimized API and web builds. Next classified the three title detail routes as generated SSG routes with on-demand params; standalone requests returned five-minute shared-cache headers and cache hits. The custom build directory was isolated, and the existing `.next-build-probe` reference in `apps/web/next-env.d.ts` was restored after the build.

Post-session playback measurement follow-up (2026-09-28): both optimized cores completed one 18-sample Watch batch after the root session-request suppression; every warm start decoded a frame. Legacy p50/p95 was 375 / 791 ms and canary was 483 / 2,154 ms. The canary tail was 2,060 ms before media `loadstart`, and fixture telemetry requests logged `ECONNRESET`. The benchmark harness now handles the canary's auto-started playback without waiting for a Play button that has become Pause. This single sequential pair is too noisy to change the player gate; repeat alternating batches on a quiet runner.

Historical redesign pass (2026-09-29): a Film Society Programme variant was applied across Home and member-facing routes, and local checks were recorded as passing. The user later confirmed the existing Cinema After Midnight screen as the visual authority; this pass is superseded. Active design rules live in `DESIGN.md`, and the Home, shell, shared palette, and visible title/profile actions are aligned to that dark/orange system. Production gates below remain open: local checks do not verify provider permissions, deployed delivery policy, real alerts, production migrations, offsite restore, Safari HLS, or production Core Web Vitals.

Provider control and playback reliability follow-up (2026-09-30): Admin can search and filter providers, edit bounded region/request limits, delete a configured provider from new playback attempts, and restore it disabled. The deletion is an audited Postgres tombstone, applied across replicas by the existing revision poll; it keeps adapter configuration and member history intact. Resolver cache keys include effective region and delivery configuration, requested audio wins over original-language fallback, repeated provider-local timeouts open its circuit, and fallback exclusions expire after their window. The first-party player reports a startup stall when metadata or first playback never arrives. Search no longer presents mood or surprise actions without backing behavior. `bun run check` passed workspace lint/typecheck, all 912 tests, and optimized API/web builds. The controlled Chrome Watch fixture suite passed all nine active scenarios, including real HLS decode, fallback, recovery, subtitle selection, and source renewal; its benchmark and visual capture cases remained explicitly skipped. Retired public-embed assertions were removed from that suite because the embed route tree intentionally returns 404. The production performance, provider-readiness, and Safari gates remain open.

Anime renewal interruption follow-up (2026-09-30): the shared player rebuilt its media engine whenever a source object changed, including a healthy renewal that only extended expiry for the same URL. Providers without an explicit upstream expiry use a conservative five-minute refresh deadline, so that metadata refresh could repeatedly interrupt an episode. Attachment now compares URL and media type and preserves the current engine while healthy; failed-engine retries and actual source changes still reload. The regression failed before the fix and passes afterward. The focused player/controller suite passed 69 tests, UI lint/typecheck passed, and the three attachment cases passed on the canary core too. Four targeted Chrome scenarios passed: unchanged-URL renewal with zero media reloads and continued playback, changed-URL renewal, paused recovery, and real HLS decode.

Rotating-URL renewal follow-up (2026-09-30): live Tatami Galaxy episode 1 on Kyren reproduced a second media load during healthy renewal because each resolve minted a different HLS URL. The same-URL guard did not cover this case. Watch now retains its attached local stream separately from the latest session lease and only consumes a renewed URL when the stream fails; provider/type/delivery changes and casting still switch immediately. A failure during renewal is retained and uses the returned lease. The Watch regression and controlled Chrome renewal scenario failed before the change and pass afterward; the browser scenario covers repeated rotating leases without reloads, captions, paused position, and using the latest URL after a real media error. Validation passed 35 Watch/coordinator tests, four targeted Chrome scenarios, lint, typecheck, and the optimized web build. On the rebuilt local app, the real Kyren episode completed two accelerated renewals with different URLs, one initial media load, zero reloads, continuous playback, and one document navigation; this is a short live-provider check, not a full-episode soak test.

Subtitle duplication follow-up (2026-09-30): Tatami Galaxy episode 1 reproduced eight Arabic rows: a saved VdrkV2 file plus its raw candidate, three RiveSubs signed copies, and three Urdu copies mislabeled as Arabic. Discovery now merges saved/raw file identities, RiveSubs deduplicates rotating signatures while retaining distinct content selectors, native Urdu/French/Bengali labels resolve correctly, and Watch selection reuses an attached file's existing track ID. All three regressions failed before their fixes. The live API now returns two Arabic choices, one each from VdrkV2 and RiveSubs; 106 subtitle tests and 27 Watch tests pass. API/web lint and type checks and the optimized web build pass. Chrome checked the real subtitle discovery against controlled local media on the rebuilt app: selecting the saved Arabic track, reopening the menu, and reloading the same browser retained two Arabic choices, one attached file, one checked option, and zero browser downloads.
