## Stage 7 — operational growth

Apogee buffering follow-up (2026-10-02): the member reported Swapped and other titles freezing despite good Wi-Fi and requested CineJoy removal. CineJoy is absent from the local and example rosters. Live Swapped playback reproduced slow CDN chunks and a stalled seek; deterministic regressions exposed missing seek watchdogs in both player cores and unnecessary same-provider renewal after delivery timeouts. Progressive HLS decoding, a larger bounded forward reserve, seek watchdog coverage, and direct fallback after timeouts improve playback while preserving the existing controls, position, and play intent. No media relay or paid service was introduced. The controlled loading comparison and live checks are recorded in the [buffering audit](./audits/apogee-buffering-2026-10-02.md). Validation passed 138 shared UI tests, 38 Watch/coordinator tests, UI/web type checks, and changed-code lint; the optimized web image was rebuilt and recreated.

Watch error follow-up (2026-10-02): reported TMDB poster CORS and FetchLoader AbortErrors led to first-party optimized posters and a rollback of progressive HLS loading, retaining the larger reserve and seek watchdog. A failing API regression repaired fallback after manual selection; three pending-seek regressions repaired destination retention through retry and replacement. All 96 UI playback, 42 Watch/coordinator, and 50 API playback tests passed, along with UI/web type checks, a focused API playback type check, changed-code lint, and the optimized web build. API was restarted and web recreated. A controlled check of the deployed player resumed at the requested position without page errors. Live provider availability remains variable and can still yield a legitimate 503. Evidence and verification limits are in the [playback error audit](./audits/playback-poster-cancellation-2026-10-02.md).

Anime recovery repair (2026-10-02): the reported anime failure reproduced on One Piece episode 1 as a 502 manifest and repeated same-provider renewals. Both previous and current HLS loader modes failed on that URL. Manifest and CORS delivery failures now skip renewal and request direct fallback after bounded engine recovery. Two regressions failed before the repair; all 44 Watch/coordinator tests, web type checking, changed-code lint, and the optimized web build passed. The deployed desktop Watch page decoded four anime episode samples without page errors, including One Piece through successful fallback. Provider adapters and saved Admin choices remain unchanged. Evidence and startup-only limits are in the [anime recovery audit](./audits/anime-manifest-recovery-2026-10-02.md).

Anime DUB repair (2026-10-02): DUB previously accepted original audio from AnimeParadise/AnimeKitsu and AnimeKai's opposite-variant fallback. Known sub-only adapters now skip DUB; fresh and cached anime sources must match the requested audio. AnimeKai stops when its requested variant is absent, and Kyren respects explicit upstream audio labels. The diagnostic DUB roster contains Kyren and AnimeKai. All 54 API playback and 119 adapter tests passed, with SDK and focused API playback type checks and changed-code lint. API was restarted; the deployed desktop DUB button requested English and decoded Attack on Titan episode 1 through Kyren, while a missing dub showed the explicit error without substituting SUB. Evidence is in the [dub-selection audit](./audits/anime-dub-selection-2026-10-02.md).

Catalog request-budget repair (2026-10-02): repeated page visits reproduced local 429 responses from the web server's shared 100/minute bucket. Public catalog reads now have a separate bounded 600/minute budget, and Home/Watch metadata uses the existing five-minute shared data cache without caching member HTML or authenticated endpoints. A seven-second AniList regression also repaired the premature six-second web cancellation with a bounded 12-second metadata deadline. Rate-limit copy no longer claims a timeout. All 35 relevant API and 15 web tests passed, with web and focused API limiter type checks, changed-file lint, API bundling, and the optimized web build. Existing API-wide retired-social/private-visibility type errors remain outside this slice. API was restarted and web recreated. The live reproduction returned 101/101 successful metadata reads; seven page samples loaded, and 20 warm Watch renders made zero extra metadata reads. Four desktop Chrome page checks passed without page errors. Evidence and measurement limits are in the [catalog rate-limit audit](./audits/catalog-rate-limit-2026-10-02.md).

Recloud anime addition (2026-10-02): the member requested PROVE's Recloud provider. An anime-only AniList-native adapter now resolves exact episodes and requested SUB/DUB lanes through bounded same-origin control requests and validated browser-direct HLS. PROVE's opposite-audio fallback is omitted. Captions and skip ranges use the existing player contract. The local row is enabled, the example remains disabled, and existing provider order/overrides are preserved. All 137 selected SDK and 55 API playback tests passed, along with SDK and focused API configuration type checks, changed-file lint, and API bundling. After API restart, deployed guest-session switches to Recloud decoded Attack on Titan episode 1 in both variants and One Piece episode 1 SUB without page errors; an earlier One Piece failure records variable upstream health. No web build was needed. Evidence and startup-only limits are in the [Recloud audit](./audits/recloud-provider-2026-10-02.md).

Add queues, worker separation, analytics storage, multiple instances, canaries, orchestration, and regions only against observed traffic or an explicit reliability requirement. The stages in [ARCHITECTURE.md](./ARCHITECTURE.md) are the trigger guide.

## Per-slice checklist

- Name the member job and canonical domain terms.
- Link the active design reference and list required states.
- Define the module interface before its implementation details.
- Use deterministic fixtures until integration is the purpose of the slice.
- Test through the same interface used by callers.
- Verify accessibility, responsiveness, and failure behavior in proportion to risk.
- Update product, domain, or architecture docs only when the underlying decision changes.
- Finish one coherent slice before expanding the next.

Anime artwork and heading (2026-10-09): Watch now separates the anime name from the episode title and keeps the header at a fixed 24px through preparation and playback. AniList covers prefer `extraLarge` with a safe large fallback and a new catalog cache generation. Both frontend builds, workspace lint/types, focused AniList and Watch tests, both player suites and desktop/mobile heading checks pass. The original frontend/API images rebuilt and are healthy; a fresh local browser confirms the heading and largest cover selection. Landscape banners remain upstream-limited. See [the audit](./audits/anime-artwork-and-heading-2026-10-09.md).

Subtitle aliases, quality and intro metadata (2026-10-09): language aliases now share one group while preserving tracks and original paging cursors. Quality uses standard tiers with cropped-width recognition and exact decoded dimensions retained in its panel; this supersedes the earlier exact-height label rule at the member’s request. Anime browse reuses the early catalog banner. Watch/embed can read optional exact intro/recap metadata through bounded fixed-host adapters, preserving provider timings and hiding Skip intro when timings are unavailable. Relevant UI/web/API tests, desktop/touch browser checks, lint/types and web/API builds passed. This slice is implemented in the workspace; containers were not rebuilt. Coverage and external cut accuracy remain upstream-dependent. See [the audit](./audits/subtitle-aliases-quality-and-intros-2026-10-09.md).

New episodes and private reminders (2026-10-09): the member job is finding exact newly aired episodes of saved series/anime and choosing optional reminders after playback has been checked. Home adds New this week after Continue watching; the member utility-row bell previews reminders, `/notifications` provides a private paginated inbox, and Settings persists an off-by-default preference. Airing and playback evidence stay distinct. Current checks open exact Watch episodes, unconfirmed shelf records open title episodes, and historical reminders warn that availability can change. Movies, future/unknown releases, series specials and completed episodes do not enter the recent shelf. Tracking is the union of Watchlist and Favorites, with opt-in/tracking timestamps preventing historical reminder backfill. Existing Cinema After Midnight typography, palette, shared cards and Player remain the visual authority.

Notifications experience recovery (2026-10-28): the inbox now preserves its last successful episode page while a next-page request fails, and Retry repeats the same cursor. All, Unread, title recommendations and social updates retain independent pagination/read behavior. Deterministic browser checks cover initial loading, failed reads, retries into honest empty states, a failed page followed by cursor retry, episode previous/next navigation, and the combined member task on dark phone and light desktop. The screenshot capture `.impeccable/review/notifications/inbox-empty-390x844.png` records the Unread empty state. All three Notifications browser checks, 11 inbox component tests, two recommendation component tests, Web typecheck and targeted ESLint passed. This closes the tested combinations; broader member, theme and viewport combinations remain in the whole-app experience ledger.

The slice includes shared release contracts, additive Postgres persistence, transactional tracking and notification insertion, a bounded catalog scheduler and a separate sandboxed Chromium worker. Checks exercise the existing media engine with decoded frames, advancing time, seek and resumed playback; they create no member progress/history. Provider eligibility, revision, region and source validation remain behind the existing adapter seam. Preferences and reminders participate in private export and account tombstoning. Architecture and composition boundaries are recorded in [ARCHITECTURE.md](./ARCHITECTURE.md#episode-releases-and-private-notifications) and [COMPONENTS.md](./COMPONENTS.md#new-episodes-and-notification-compositions).

Workspace lint and type checking passed, along with 1,490 workspace tests (492 API, 20 contracts, 47 relay, 271 provider SDK, 18 release worker, 211 shared UI and 431 web). Two new catalog-adapter, two new mutation and one telemetry-startup regression passed separately; sixteen script checks and thirty disposable-database integration tests passed. The final three real-browser scenarios all passed through the production web image and fixture gateway, covering dark/light desktop/mobile surfaces, successful decode/play/seek/resume, broken segments, frozen video, failed seeking and unauthorized probe access. The sandboxed container with the actual proxy also submitted evidence with 174 decoded frames, 5.004853 seconds advancing, a completed seek and 2.009903 seconds resumed. API/web/worker images built and infrastructure validation passed, including seventeen production alert rules. Captures are retained under `.impeccable/review/releases/`. The independent Impeccable finish review disposition is ship; the documenter preserved root DESIGN and its sidecar.

Local runtime rollout completed against the existing managed database and localhost:8080 deployment. The named `/backups/pre-episode-releases-20261009.dump` snapshot was verified; only the three release migrations were applied through an isolated migration directory. The concurrent `20261009170000_public_member_discovery` migration was left pending. Compatible API/web/gateway images and the non-root sandboxed worker are running, tracking is enabled through the private ignored `.env.releases.local`, member preferences remain off by default, and live catalog refreshes reported success with zero queue backlog. Production readiness/catalog/private-route smoke passed. Runtime metric initialization was corrected to occur after SDK startup, with its source regression passing; the correction was isolated onto the previously validated API image to avoid including concurrent public-profile implementation changes. Previous API/web images are retained for rollback. The [release runbook](./runbooks/episode-releases.md) records activation and recovery. No remote VPS rollout or universal provider/region/audio availability is claimed.

A later workspace recheck encountered concurrent public-profile type errors in `identity/memory-repository.ts` and `member-data/public-members.ts`. These are outside this slice; they were preserved and excluded from the validated release runtime rather than reported as passing. The full-workspace pass above predates those edits. Release-focused checks and the deployed smoke remain passing. Prometheus now exposes the release queue-age histogram with the expected names; the local four-rule and production seventeen-rule alert files validate, and the local rule reload completed. Temporary browser/API/database fixture services were stopped after verification.


Public profiles, following and People search (2026-10-09): the member job is deliberately publishing taste, discovering people and following public members. Profiles stay private until explicitly published; the editor explains shared identity, Favorites, all ratings and all reviews beside the Public/Private control. Lists retain their own private default and become visitor-accessible only while their owner is public. Separate visitor projections exclude location, private collections/statistics, tags, import metadata, history, progress and account details. Visitors get Overview, Ratings, Reviews, Lists, Followers and Following; owner collections retain their existing behavior. Canonical username aliases, Share, noindex, uniform unavailable states and no-store reads remain in place.

People search uses the existing PostgreSQL database with partial pg_trgm indexes, case-insensitive substring matching, exact-username-first ranking and scoped bounded pagination. Both search surfaces include People; its requests bypass TMDB and AniList, retain overlay debounce/cancellation/keyboard behavior, and expose honest empty, loading and retry states. Follow/unfollow reuse the unique relationship constraint with authenticated origin-protected idempotent writes. Guest follow from People search returns to the current query after sign-in; other profile follow links return to that profile. Sign-in never creates a follow. Privacy changes preserve relationships; visitors see only active public members and counts, while owners can unfollow neutral retained-private placeholders. No additional paid service is required. Feeds, messages, follow requests and follow notifications remain excluded.

Observed verification passed 500 API tests, 22 contract tests, 57 relevant profile/search/list frontend tests plus two publishing save/retry tests, eleven existing member-data database tests and five public-member database tests. The public-member tests cover privacy combinations, aliases/deletion, visibility reversal, retained relationships, concurrent writes, scoped pagination, indexed search and one-time legacy normalization preserving content/follows. Database verification used disposable `consumit_social_test`, never the managed member database. API lint/types/bundle and web lint/type checks passed at their recorded checkpoints; web lint carried one unrelated concurrent Watch dependency warning. The latest optimized production fixture build passed compilation, TypeScript and static generation. Three Chrome browser scenarios passed for guest authentication, desktop/mobile publishing/search/follow/share/visitor navigation and failure recovery; the desktop/mobile matrix passed again to capture the list editor without rebuilding app source. Follow/sign-in targets measured at least 38px, and the browser matrix found no client page errors or horizontal overflow. Twelve focused frontend tests and changed-file lint passed after the final UI fixes.

Captures are retained under `.impeccable/review/social/`. The independent full finish review identified three fixes; its subsequent verdict scored all three resolved and returned ship at that fix-list scope. It did not independently certify backend security. The final design documentation handoff preserved root DESIGN.md and its sidecar after comparing the finished extension with the incumbent system. Its obsolete COMPONENTS follow-retirement sentence was reconciled with this explicitly authorized reactivation. Pre-existing primary-button and sidecar example drift was reported and left untouched. See [the audit](./audits/public-profiles-2026-10-09.md) and [rollout runbook](./runbooks/public-profiles.md).

This slice is implemented and verified in the workspace. `20261009170000_public_member_discovery` has not been applied to the managed database, and the running API/web were not rebuilt for this slice. Concurrent first-visit preferences also require their own pending migration, so the shared checkout must ship as a verified compatible migration/API/web bundle; a generic migrate deploy must not silently apply unrelated pending work. Preserve the active episode-release worker and ignored deployment configuration. No local runtime or remote VPS rollout is claimed for public profiles.

Welcome Guide and Viewing Preferences (2026-10-09): the viewer job is choosing how Watch starts without making setup or account creation a prerequisite. The optional three-step guide edits Viewing Preferences, allows up to four deliberate member Favorites, then explains Search/Watch, Watchlist/Continue watching and new-episode reminders. Guests retain device choices and can continue without an account; verified account creation preserves its original internal destination. Settings reuses the editor and can reopen the guide. An unconfigured account can explicitly copy guest device choices into its draft before Save. `playbackConfigured` distinguishes actual playback changes from guide dismissal/completion, so guide-only records never imply consent to import playback choices.

The slice includes strict complete-read/partial-write contracts, authenticated private preference persistence, account export/deletion and member-scoped device fallback/retry. Watch applies preferences before initial resolution and later player choices save semantic languages/resolution rather than engine track IDs. Anime keeps explicit SUB/DUB consent and unavailable-dub recovery. Direct Watch never displays the browsing prompt. The prompt is compiled with `NEXT_PUBLIC_CONSUMIT_WELCOME_ENABLED`, default false; direct guide routes remain available. Optional Home/Ready community cards use the supplied Discord and Reddit destinations with independent Home dismissal. Existing Cinema After Midnight, shared form/title components and the Consumit Player are preserved; root `DESIGN.md` and its sidecar receive no extension-specific rewrite.

Final verification for this slice passed all 449 web tests across 78 files, after the player and preference-bootstrap regressions. Earlier full workspace checkpoints passed 503 API, 24 contracts, 47 relay, 272 provider SDK, 18 release worker and 216 shared UI tests; these are separate recorded checkpoints, not one combined final suite. All seven workspaces passed final lint and type checking, sixteen script checks passed, and infrastructure checks passed Cloudflare/AWS configuration and seventeen alert rules. Two integration tests passed against disposable `consumit_experience_test`, covering concurrent partial writes, member isolation, restart persistence, export and tombstone/late-write serialization. The final optimized production Chrome fixture run passed all five scenarios in 40.4 seconds (`local/experience-production-e2e-verified.log`): guest skip/completion and destination retention, direct Watch preference application, member Favorites/write retry/cross-device restore, explicit device-choice import after signup, and the complete desktop/mobile dark/light matrix. All twenty-four captures under `.impeccable/review/experience/` were refreshed on 2026-10-10.

The independent full Impeccable finish reviewer returned ship after inspecting all twenty-four fresh captures, with no material fixes required. This review covers the guide, form, optional Favorites, community cards, Settings and direct-Watch preference handoff. It does not certify backend security or establish live-provider playback coverage. A later behavior-only Watch guard skips an empty preference patch when the player callback intentionally filters an anime-only audio event out of movie/series preferences; the strict API already rejects empty PATCH bodies. No markup or visual treatment changed, so the twenty-four captures remain applicable. The guard-only reviewer follow-up returned ship/resolved; all forty-six Watch/Anime tests, final web lint and the optimized image build including Next.js TypeScript passed (`local/experience-player-final.log`, `local/experience-web-lint-guard.log`, `local/experience-web-image-guard.log`). The documenter rechecked the guard against shared contracts and preserved incumbent design files and previously reported unrelated drift.

Onboarding keyboard context (2026-10-28): the shared GuideFrame focuses the active step heading on entry and when Save or Back changes steps, with the existing focus color shown for keyboard navigation. The member browser journey first failed on missing focus, then passed after the fix, including all three headings and Back. Profile Network tab (2026-10-09, at the member's request): the separate Followers and Following profile tabs merge into one Network tab. `/profile/{username}/network` reuses the shared Tabs component for an internal Followers/Following view switch driven by a shareable view query and delegates paginated member rows to the existing public-collection composition; legacy `/followers` and `/following` profile URLs redirect into the matching Network view, and profile follower/following stat links open the same views. Relationship collections, counts, privacy rules, member rows and follow actions are unchanged; the retired Circle-style Network feed stays retired. Web lint, types and the full web unit suite (453 tests, including the updated profile routing, navigation and public-profile tests) passed; containers were not rebuilt. Product, design, component and architecture language for the tab is updated at its single source of truth in PRODUCT.md, DESIGN.md, COMPONENTS.md, ARCHITECTURE.md and the CONTEXT.md glossary.

Saved subtitle activation correction (2026-10-10): the viewer reported subtitles Off across titles after saving Arabic then English with subtitles enabled. A Watch regression reproduced no discovery before Play/menu opening, and both controller regressions reproduced English fallback remaining selected after Arabic arrived. Enabled intent now starts title-scoped, cancelable discovery when a direct session becomes ready. Automatic preparation searches ahead of an existing lower-priority fallback; both controllers reconcile late tracks in saved language order while preserving a chosen release within that language and intentional Off. Automatic fallback never writes a new preference or turns the saved switch off. Source/lease replacement keeps the title's discovery instead of starting another search. Provider-frame limitations and Player markup/styles remain unchanged.

All 220 shared UI tests and 56 relevant Watch/experience web tests passed, as did UI/web lint and type checks and the optimized web image build. Two deterministic Chrome tests prove Arabic auto-selection, failed-Arabic English fallback, visible caption cues, unchanged saved priority and manual Off. The built local site originally failed the same automatic-Arabic check after 45 seconds; after rollout it passed real subtitle discovery/preparation with controlled native video, retained enabled Arabic/English intent and saved manual Off (`local/subtitle-live-before.log`, `local/subtitle-live-after.log`). Normal gateway production smoke passed. Web now runs `consumit-web:subtitles` (`sha256:ab906d3d81e3ffff41aabedec0529dbb530bb7258c3c218377b4cf88ffead426`) through `local/subtitle-deploy.compose.yaml` after the existing experience/home overlays; API and web remain healthy. This behavior-only correction required no migration or API/worker deployment and preserves concurrent Home/profile work. Real subtitle evidence covers the tested movie, not guaranteed language availability across the catalog or live streaming-provider decoding.

Phone interface refinement (2026-10-12): the member jobs are browsing/choosing titles, Search, personal navigation, rating and settings on a phone. The shipped shell uses a closed-by-default native navigation sheet, compact utility row and catalog tabs. Home's phone hero is bounded, Search has a full-height phone composition with explicit dismissal and focus containment, portrait captions wrap below artwork, shared touch controls have 44px targets, form text is 16px, Select popups stay inside the viewport, and phone ratings expose ten numbered native-radio choices. Safe-area padding protects the phone shell and Settings save bar. Existing catalog/member flows, desktop icon rail and Consumit Player remain intact. Three Chrome browser scenarios passed at 320px, 390px and landscape; the final relevant web run passed 90 tests, shared UI passed 236 tests at its checkpoint and focused selection/rating/card checks passed after the final updates. Web/UI lint and types, the final optimized web build and diff checks passed. No deployment or physical-device certification is claimed. See [the phone audit](./audits/phone-interface-2026-10-12.md).


## Incremental rebuild architecture (2026-10-13)

The member job is preserving browse, sign-in, Watch, resume and Save while moving provider/background work into separate Node processes. [ADR 0010](./adr/0010-node-processes-and-durable-jobs.md) records ownership and [the dated audit](./audits/rebuild-architecture-2026-10-13.md) records actual preservation, runtime, PostgreSQL/queue/browser/load/restore evidence. The immutable candidate was staged privately on the VPS with fixture data. Desktop/touch-emulation member flows passed; VPS load delivered 300 successful requests per run, including progress writes with playback stopped. Queue recovery, graceful worker shutdown and traces across the three services were observed. Production promotion remains pending physical phones, field playback/Web Vitals, representative workload and total recurring-cost gates; the member deferred phone checks. The current VPS bill is reported as $15 monthly. One million monthly active members is a future capacity destination, with progress and media delivery accounted separately.

At the subsequent explicit deployment request on 2026-10-14, the Node product API, queue and general worker were activated, with a 1% playback canary and compatible Node fallback. A fresh off-host backup preceded the two additive migrations. The live Node member checks and public production smoke passed; movie/series/anime resolution passed through playback. The live provider configuration exposed missing canonical-resolver injection in adapter initialization; playback v7 fixes it with twelve-adapter/series-IMDb regression coverage. The existing web/PostgreSQL/provider configuration and authentication secrets were preserved, and diagnostic records were removed. Release checks remain disabled as before. The [audit](./audits/rebuild-architecture-2026-10-13.md#production-canary-activation--2026-10-14) records exact versions, measured results, rollout recovery and remaining full-promotion gates. Deferred checks remain incomplete; the canary does not establish million-member capacity.

Arabic caption and player placement correction (2026-10-18): A resolver regression reproduced Arabic WebVTT bypassing the existing byte decoder on its direct-track path; Arabic VTT is now normalized within its validated provider scope, with the direct candidate retained if conversion cannot complete. Browser uploads and cue synchronization decode subtitle bytes consistently. Regression tests cover Windows-1256 decoding through the server cache and local upload. The 133 API subtitle tests and 230 UI playback tests passed; the final preparation fallback additionally passed all 68 tests in `playback.test.tsx`. A deterministic Chrome run passed at 1920×822, 1440×900, 1280×548, 1024×600, 390×844, 320×720 and 844×390, with no overflow or browser page errors and equal phone control spacing. Targeted API/UI lint and type checks passed, as did optimized local API and web image builds. The rebuilt local API and web containers are healthy; `http://localhost:8080/watch/movie/550` and the API readiness endpoint return HTTP 200. Local image IDs: web `sha256:d32cccd5d7f5088593f482552f9ff524b27add0ca838832775627ccaadef75cf`; API `sha256:2904d9e6bc07501811d506b32803744be4c4038b739c64b809230db97b586a17`. No production services were changed. See [the player caption and control audit](./audits/player-caption-and-control-review-2026-10-18.md).

PROVE-derived provider check (2026-10-19): refreshed both Cloudflare Resolver Workers so the current compiled adapters are available in production. Existing Filmu/Movy/CineJoy worker resolution plus sampled media checks remained healthy. Aether resolves but TNMR media returns 403; BingeBang YMovies, Cinecat and StreamingUnity remain unavailable from production resolver egress, although local/PROVE adapters resolve. No new rows were enabled and no playback success is claimed. See [the provider resolver audit](./audits/provider-resolver-current-adapters-2026-10-19.md).

Home list discovery and title quick actions (2026-10-19): added “New from your network” and “Popular with members” public-list shelves, ranking the latter by anonymous saved-list bookmarks with a five-member threshold and returning no save counts or identities. Added a three-dot title menu across shared title cards for watchlist, watched, likes, list add, rating/review, details and playback actions. The menu portal inherits active FilScreen theme tokens. Web typecheck and optimized web/API image builds passed. The local Docker deployment uses the explicit local-Postgres Compose overlay; API and web return healthy, Home and `/v1/lists/discovery` return HTTP 200, and discovery is private/no-store. The local database currently has no qualifying public lists, so the shelves correctly stay hidden. Neon and remote services were not changed.

Title-card quick-action visual refinement (2026-10-19): the ellipsis control now uses a small theme-colored disc within a 44px touch target, and the popover uses a compact raised surface, editorial title marker and tighter action rows. Phone rows retain 44px targets; the menu flips above cards when there is not enough room below. Web typecheck and the optimized web image build passed. The web container is healthy on the local Postgres Compose stack, Home returns HTTP 200, and the menu was reviewed in the local browser. No tests were run for this visual-only refinement; no API, database, or remote services were changed.

Provider production promotion (2026-10-20): the production roster grew from 73 to 80 rows, retaining existing rows and overrides and enabling the seven active local additions: Vidlink, Cinecat, Aether, FMFAU, StreamingUnity, AuroraScreen and MovieNight. The public playback provider list now returns all seven additions (18 providers visible for the sampled movie). Anixo now emits one stable server source. MegaVid uses the reviewed relay policy for its rotating `s-*.megavid.se` hosts. KotoTV's API/playback v4 adapter retries transient 5xx responses at search, watch, embed and HLS stages; live diagnostics showed the VPS's direct egress repeatedly receiving 503 from the watch page while the production Resolver Worker returned a valid source, so KotoTV was explicitly routed through that Worker in both production playback paths. The existing playback canary remains at 1%; no database migration ran. The Media Relay Worker deployed as `a04753f7-8a06-4c99-a6e8-494933d3f8bd`; Resolver Workers deployed as `44b6c675-24d6-4fc0-9fd2-50779f20d396` and `a5bbb8e6-34c0-4503-bdc5-b80a34894794`. API, API legacy and playback run `provider-fixes-20261020-v4`; readiness returned 200. Production smoke returned 200 for Anixo and MegaVid, and MegaVid's relayed HLS manifest returned 200 with the expected CORS origin. KotoTV resolved and its direct HLS manifest returned 200 in the API legacy check; the playback-service check resolved but the upstream manifest intermittently returned 503, and a later public resolve sample also returned 503. The tested Resolver Worker route succeeds, but KotoTV remains intermittent at the source and media layers. One post-rollout sample of the seven additions resolved Vidlink, Cinecat and FMFAU; Aether, StreamingUnity, AuroraScreen and MovieNight returned `PLAYBACK_UNAVAILABLE` at sample time. The rows are enabled in production, but source availability remains dependent on upstream services and varies by title/time. Adapter tests passed (KotoTV 11/11, provider SDK adapter suite 156/156 before the final retry-stage addition); configured-provider tests passed 32/32. Wrangler dry-runs passed before the Worker deployments. No broader availability is inferred from these sampled titles.

Provider production recheck (2026-10-20): `provider-fixes-20261020-v5` adds the canonical title metadata omitted from AuroraScreen, MovieNight and StreamingUnity Resolver requests, and both Resolver Workers were refreshed. The 31-case public sample had 17 source and first-media-prefix successes; alternate title checks show VidLink, Cinecat and KoToTV misses are title/episode-specific, while MegaVid and Anixo resolved the checked anime sample. The VPS and Resolver Worker both remain unable to reach AuroraScreen, MovieNight and StreamingUnity because their upstream lookup endpoints return HTTP 403 from production egress. Rigel and Aether also returned no source for the rechecked movie/series samples. No upstream access restrictions were bypassed. Tests, worker versions, and per-provider evidence are in [the production recheck audit](./audits/provider-production-recheck-2026-10-20.md).

Playback recovery and integrated Watch (2026-10-24): a shared `PlaybackRecoveryViewModel` and `PlayerRecoveryPanel` now cover automatic source search, source exhaustion, startup/interruption/provider-frame failures, and manual source choice. Choosing a source aborts the active automatic request before opening Sources, so options become actionable. Successful retry retains the exact recording, checkpoint and play intent; a failed manual choice preserves a healthy source and gives a dismissible notice. Provider frames use the Consumit Watch shell and capability copy; CSP frame blocks and the existing 20-second no-load timeout enter recovery, while iframe load still does not assert playback. Deterministic focused checks passed: UI recovery/source action 6/6, provider-frame 8/8, and Watch recovery/continuity 14/14. UI and web type checks passed. Changed-file ESLint passed with three existing hook-dependency warnings in `watch-playback.tsx`. The Impeccable scan found no recovery-panel issue and flagged one existing focus-color advisory in the shared stylesheet. A deterministic browser fixture was visually reviewed at 1440×900, 390×844, 320×720 and 844×390; keyboard focus reached Cancel then Choose a source and the accessibility tree exposed searching/failure status. The complete UI playback file still has two unrelated subtitle/caption fixture failures, and the complete Watch file has six unrelated subtitle/relay/progress fixture failures; the targeted recovery cases pass. The development Watch route hit a Next dev error boundary (`frame.join is not a function`), so visual review used the direct recovery fixture. No image rebuild, production rollout, API or database change was made.

Guest episode completion and return path (2026-10-28): a browser journey starts at series detail, selects the finale and ends its fixture video naturally. The persistent status announces completion, while a visible finale group provides a 44px `Return to series` link and no member-only actions. The test checks 390px and 320px widths, viewport fit and clearance above the player control row. Inspection exposed a shared-layer overlap; watch status moved from the bottom controls wrapper to the player stage. The focused finale browser test passes. Watch playback/screen tests pass 68/68, shared player-room tests pass 6/6, subtitle/player contract tests pass 13/13, and the player-room browser suite passes 7 with one existing touch viewport matrix skipped. Web typecheck passes; changed Watch source lint has three existing hook-dependency warnings. Screenshots are in `.impeccable/review/watch-journey/guest-series-finale-390x844.png` and `guest-series-finale-320x844.png`. Episode return-context refinement (2026-10-28): the series finale action carries season and episode context to `/series/[id]?season=…&episode=…#episode-…-…`. The route accepts only a season and episode present in catalog data; the episode list restores the containing page, scrolls the exact row below the app header and transfers keyboard focus to it. The same guest finale browser journey verifies keyboard activation, Season 2, page 2 and episode 10 in the viewport with visible focus at 390px and 320px, without horizontal overflow. Catalog detail/episode component tests pass 15/15, web typecheck passes, and the shared player component lints clean. No persistence or API contract changed.

Player recovery viewport correction (2026-10-28): a browser matrix found that the recovery modal was anchored to the video-only stage and its actions were clipped by the player controls at phone heights. `PlayerRecoveryPanel` now covers the full player shell so its background is inert and visually dimmed while its modal remains fully readable. Browser checks verify the overlay covers the shell and its actions have 44px targets at 390×844, 320×720 and 844×390 on desktop and touch Chrome. The full player-room suite passes 9 tests with one existing duplicate touch viewport matrix skipped. Paused-position recovery and manual-source recovery pass in both browser projects; the latter confirms the 0:12 checkpoint and actionable Sources drawer. Recovery-panel/player-room UI tests pass 11/11. The stale manual-recovery browser assertion was updated to match the current alertdialog and source-choice flow. No playback contract, progress ownership or provider behavior changed.

Separate-audio sync control refinement (2026-10-28): the active separate-audio panel's `Reset audio sync` relied on a panel-wide 44px button rule and had no local keyboard-focus treatment. It is now a rounded 44px-minimum control with the existing orange focus ring. The shared `SeparateAudioSyncControl` keeps the adjustment, warning and reset action together; a focused component regression verifies sync-change and reset callbacks, target classes and focus styling. The control-only test passes within the player contract suite; real-media audio decoding remains outside this check. No audio-selection or playback behavior changed.

Watch panel target audit (2026-10-28): a browser check now verifies the three subtitle-sync actions and TV-casting recheck action have at least 44px targets. Their local styles now declare that same minimum explicitly; subtitle sync keyboard focus is confirmed in a 390×844 phone capture at `.impeccable/review/watch-journey/subtitle-sync-focus-phone.png`. The nested-panel Watch flow passes on desktop and touch Chrome. No subtitle discovery, casting availability, or media playback behavior changed.

Search scope flow (2026-10-28): the full-page All/Movies/Series/Anime selection is URL-backed; movie and series searches and short-query discovery now request the matching catalog kind, while anime remains AniList-backed. A new query preserves the scope, empty filtered results do not promote a title from another kind, and filter navigation retains keyboard focus. Search screen/page unit tests pass 10/10, the phone browser flow passes at 390×844 including query submit and two Back steps, web typecheck passes, and changed Search source lint passes. The inspected phone capture is `.impeccable/review/search-scope-390x844.png`; the route contract is recorded in Product. People results, broader empty/error/pagination behavior and destination retention remain open in the experience ledger.

Search source recovery (2026-10-28): All scope now reads movie/series and Anime sources concurrently and independently. A failure from either source keeps the other results visible with a source-specific retry; single-filter catalog errors retain the query and filter with an inline retry. Anime empty, unavailable and sign-in-required states stay distinct, and Anime sign-in returns to the query. The hero overlay now follows the active theme, and the sign-in link has a measured 44px target at 320px. All three Search test files pass 27/27, web typecheck passes, changed app-source ESLint has no errors (the repository reports its existing missing-pages configuration warning; the Playwright file has no matching ESLint config), and the 390×844 Search browser flow passes through filter, query, Back and Anime sign-in return. The light 320×720 state also passes the WCAG 2.2 AA axe scan and has no horizontal overflow. Dark 390×844 and light 320×720 captures are `.impeccable/review/search-anime-sign-in-390x844.png` and `.impeccable/review/search-anime-sign-in-light-320x720.png`. Remaining People keyboard, direct browser no-match and destination-retention states stay open in the coverage ledger.

People no-match recovery (2026-10-28): a successful empty People search now has regression coverage for the tailored no-match message, curator suggestions and the Explore collections destination. A failed suggestion read retains the search query and exposes a refresh retry; the Search page test confirms it requests suggestions on no-match and passes the failure state to the screen. People rows put the profile link before the follow action, and guest follow links meet the 44px touch minimum; the row sequence test passes through two curators. The Search directory passes 28/28 tests, web typecheck passes, and changed-source ESLint passes with the repository's existing missing-pages warning. Direct browser captures for People no-match/suggestions remain open in the coverage ledger.

Title-card keyboard order (2026-10-28): the shared `WatchCard` now places its title destination before optional Save and More actions in DOM tab order, without changing their overlay positions. A failing UI regression reproduced actions before title; the component suite passes 4/4 after the reorder. The Search browser sequence passes from People filter through two rows in title → Save → More order, and both Search browser journeys pass. The inspected phone capture is `.impeccable/review/search-keyboard-results-390x844.png`.

People search continuity (2026-10-28): cursor-page failures keep prior public results, focus the Retry action, and preserve the same cursor. Successful paging announces loaded/shown counts, labels the visible count precisely, and restores loaded results after opening a profile and using Back. The short-lived session cache validates its public-summary schema and scopes entries to the query and current viewer. People-screen tests pass, the focused production-build browser flow passes through failure → retry → second curator → profile → Back, and all four Social browser journeys pass across desktop and phone. Web typecheck and People source lint pass. The broader no-match/suggestions recovery and full keyboard traversal remain in the Search ledger.

Global keyboard entry (2026-10-28): the root skip link now receives the first forward Tab from `body` and sends focus to the route's single main landmark. A multi-shell browser regression passes on Search, Sign in, Help, guest Settings, onboarding, Watch and visitor profile, alongside the original Browse check; it verifies the visible phone target is at least 44px. The inspected Watch capture is `.impeccable/review/accessibility/watch-skip-link-focus-390x844.png`. Accessibility browser checks pass 2/2 and Web typecheck passes. Targeted source ESLint reports no errors; its existing Next rule warns that the repo has no `pages` directory, and the browser test is outside that ESLint configuration. This covers global page entry; route-specific controls and screen-reader behavior remain in the full app audit.

Movie return and resume (2026-10-28): the discovery → title → Watch journey now covers leaving the active player through its accessible Back link, seeing `Continue watching` on the title, and resuming at the stored checkpoint. The focused Chrome browser check passes and confirms both return/continue targets meet 44px, the resumed 390×844 page has no horizontal overflow, and the capture is `.impeccable/review/watch-journey/return-and-resume-390x844.png`. The deterministic guest progress store is the persistence boundary here; member database persistence and other Watch return cases remain separate coverage.

Approved experience seams (2026-10-28): a combined Chrome run passes the three browser journeys agreed for this whole-app review: discovery → title → Watch return/resume, profile → review → Community, and signup → welcome/preferences → member profile. Each uses the controlled experience fixture; these journeys complement the broader route-by-route ledger and do not close its remaining state coverage.

Pending signup email correction (2026-10-28): a 320×844 browser regression exposed an unclear detour: the username reserved by a pending signup looked generically unavailable after changing email, and the submit button still appeared active. The pending notice now names the original email and username and explains that another email requires a different username. Send verification code disables after a confirmed unavailable result and returns when an available username is chosen; the test also protects the blur-triggered submit path while availability is still checking. The phone flow completes signup with the corrected email, replacement username and original return destination. The focused browser test passes; capture: `.impeccable/review/account-verification/reserved-username-email-switch-320x844.png`. No reservation contract or API behavior changed.

Inline signup expiry recovery (2026-10-28): the fixture showed that an expired challenge cannot be resent because the server requires registration details again. Inline verification now labels its retry action `Start signup again` after code rejection and restores the retained fields. A fresh availability read clears the stale pending notice only when the server releases the username; if it remains reserved, the existing route back to pending verification stays available. The phone browser journey expires a code, restarts with the same email and username, completes a new code, and reaches the original destination. The focused Chrome test passes with capture `.impeccable/review/account-verification/inline-expired-code-320x844.png`. No API or reservation contract changed.

Anime title details now retry optional related-title metadata in place. ## Watch checkpoint sync recovery — local, 2026-10-28

A failed member checkpoint write already leaves the latest position on device, but paused or completed playback had no explicit way to sync it again; the status claimed an automatic retry even though the periodic retry only runs while playing. Watch now offers `Retry account sync`, with the message kept separate from the action so the status remains understandable to assistive technology. A failed retry leaves the action available, and success clears the notice. At 320×720 the toast wraps into a readable message and action row, the retry target measures at least 44px, and the document has no horizontal overflow. The focused component regression and deterministic Chrome browser flow pass through two failed account writes followed by success; the toast subtree passes axe WCAG 2.2 AA. Web typecheck and changed Watch ESLint pass. Capture: `.impeccable/review/watch-journey/progress-sync-retry-320x720.png`. The browser fixture verifies local behavior only, not production database availability.

The adjacent `Retry preference sync` action also had only the shared small button height. Its component regression now checks a 44px minimum while preserving the existing recoverable preference write and retry behavior.

## Settings account deletion recovery — local, 2026-10-28

The Settings deletion review names the profile, reviews, ratings, saved titles, lists, Watch History and playback progress that are removed, says the username remains reserved, and directs members to request an export first. A current password and explicit acknowledgement are required. While deletion is pending, fields and actions lock and progress is announced. A failed request keeps the password and acknowledgement available, focuses the theme-aware inline error, and permits retry; success clears the session and returns the member to guest Settings.

The focused 320×720 light-theme browser journey holds the first DELETE pending, verifies repeat submission is blocked, returns a controlled 503, checks retained inputs and focused recovery, then returns a successful response with the session cookie cleared. The action targets measure at least 44px, there is no horizontal overflow, and axe reports no WCAG 2.2 AA violations. The failure state exposed low-contrast danger text; the theme-specific danger token fixes it. Component tests pass 10/10, Web typecheck passes, and targeted ESLint passes with the repository's existing Pages-directory warning. Inspected capture: `.impeccable/review/settings/account-delete-retry-320x720-light.png`. The browser intercepts the deletion response; no real member record is changed.

## Title rating read and write recovery — local, 2026-10-28

The title rating waits for the saved value before enabling choices, preventing a late read from overwriting a member’s new choice. Read failures offer Retry rating. Save failures say the rating could not be confirmed, keep the previous displayed score, preserve the requested score for Retry save, and lock other choices until the result is retried. Clicking a star clears its hover preview until the parent confirms the saved value.

The deterministic 320×700 browser flow forces the first rating write to fail, checks the unchanged score state, 44px retry target, viewport fit and axe WCAG 2.2 AA, then returns a successful retry and confirms the requested rating is selected. The fixture controls the rating responses; no production rating write is claimed. Rating-input tests pass 4/4, rating/member-action/title-detail tests pass 26/26, Web and UI typechecks pass, and changed-source ESLint passes with the existing Pages-directory warning. Capture: `.impeccable/review/title-journey/rating-retry-320x700.png`.

## Title shelf read and write recovery — local, 2026-10-28

The title Watch/Like/Save shelf waits for its saved state before accepting changes. A failed read offers Retry shelf; a failed write restores the previous displayed state and keeps the exact requested toggle for Retry shelf update. All three toggles lock together during reads and writes. Disabled actions show the actual stored label, without the hover preview implying a change was saved.

The deterministic 320×700 browser flow pauses the first Watch write, checks the pending lock, returns a controlled 503, confirms the previous state and clear retry action, then returns success for the same PUT. The retry target is 44px and fully in view, the page has no horizontal overflow, and the shelf passes axe WCAG 2.2 AA. The flow passes; the member-action/rating/title-detail component suite passes 26/26 and Web typecheck passes. ESLint reports only the repository's existing Pages-directory warning. Capture: `.impeccable/review/title-journey/shelf-retry-320x700.png`. Responses are fixture-controlled; no production write is claimed.

## Review like recovery — local, 2026-10-28

A failed like or unlike keeps the previous displayed state and exact requested choice. Retry sends that same idempotent choice; failure focuses the retry action, and confirmation restores focus to Like while announcing the saved result. The 320×720 profile → review browser flow forces 503 then 204, checks the 44px retry, focus restoration, no horizontal overflow and axe WCAG 2.2 AA. Capture: `.impeccable/review/review-journey/like-retry-320x720.png`. Browser responses are fixture-controlled; no production write is claimed.

## Review publication access and destructive-action recovery — local, 2026-10-28

Stable review pages are public when an active author's Reviews section is visible; signed-in readers still respect blocks, and authors retain access to their own hidden reviews. API integration coverage records this audience rule. The database integration suite could not run here because the Docker daemon is unavailable; its dedicated Postgres test service is ephemeral and was not substituted with the configured application database.

Comment deletion now asks before soft-removal. If the server commits the delete but its response is lost, the dialog keeps the comment visible and the owner can retry the same DELETE; repeated deletes return success without incrementing revision again. The deterministic 320×720 flow checks cancel-focus return, failed-write retention, retry focus, a 44px action, axe WCAG 2.2 AA and no overflow.

Reports now send a member-scoped idempotency key. The same key and payload produce at most one report, while a changed payload under that key conflicts; retries preserve the reason and key. A 320×720 report/block flow checks same-key report recovery, retained report reason, explicit block retry, focus, 44px actions, axe WCAG 2.2 AA and no overflow. Report API payloads are fixture-controlled; the PostgreSQL idempotency integration case is authored but could not run without Docker. Focused API route tests pass 5/5, Social component tests pass 4/4, and Web/API typechecks pass. Five focused browser checks pass across the consolidated and follow-up runs: guest title → archive → stable public review; like recovery; comment-post recovery; report/block recovery; and ambiguous comment-delete recovery. The first combined run exposed a composer-focus race; focus now returns after saving ends and the textarea is enabled. The expanded title fixture also exposed an ambiguous test locator; it now selects the first review link and verifies the intended stable page. The delete retry fixture simulates commit followed by a lost response. No production write or deployment is claimed.

## Review comment recovery — local, 2026-10-28

A failed comment or reply no longer offers a feed reload as its recovery action. The composer retains the exact body and spoiler setting, retries with the same idempotency key, and keeps edits disabled while a request is pending. Failure focuses a specific retry action; success announces the result and returns focus to the composer. The 320×720 profile → review → conversation browser flow forces 503 then 204, checks the saved spoiler remains masked until disclosed, the 44px retry, focus, no horizontal overflow and axe WCAG 2.2 AA. Capture: `.impeccable/review/review-journey/comment-retry-320x720.png`. The combined profile/review/community browser checks pass 3/3; focused Social component tests pass 4/4 and Web typecheck passes. Changed-source ESLint has no errors; it prints the repo's existing Pages-directory warning. Browser responses are fixture-controlled; no production write is claimed.

## Watch caption continuity across lease renewals — local, 2026-10-28

Renewing the same provider rendition used to mint a new `videoVersion` when the adapter had no explicit recording key. Watch interpreted that as a new caption context and reset the selected track to Off while leaving the attached video running. Same-source lease renewals now retain `videoVersion`; changed source identity and a changed explicit recording key still create a new version. A focused API regression covers refresh identity, and the browser renewal journey verifies the selected prepared caption remains active across repeated renewals while paused position and playback stay intact. The subtitle discovery journey now follows a genuinely selectable release after the first page’s already-prepared duplicates are represented by the existing tracks.

## Watch lease renewal after a hidden tab — local simulation, 2026-10-29

When a viewer returns to Watch with a direct source URL expiring, the screen now attaches the renewed URL and preserves its paused position. Same-source relayed HLS remains attached so its engine can adopt the new lease in place; its callback can use the attached source before React has run the effect that stores a fresher lease. A direct source with a rotated URL must reattach to avoid continuing against an expired address.

The 320×720 browser flow simulates a hidden → visible transition, verifies the `/refresh` response URL is the URL attached by the player, keeps playback paused at 0:06, and checks no recovery alert, no horizontal overflow and axe WCAG 2.2 AA in desktop and touch Chrome. The focused flow passes 2/2. The existing unchanged-URL relay renewal flow also passes 2/2, WatchPlayback component tests pass 69/69, and the complete Web suite passes 688/688 across 106 files. Web and Playwright TypeScript checks pass; changed-source ESLint reports only the repository's existing missing Pages-directory warning. Inspected capture: `.impeccable/review/watch-journey/visible-tab-source-renewal-320x720.png`. The local fixture controls expiry; this does not claim production provider or database persistence.

## Watch checkpoint flush on in-app departure — local, 2026-10-29

Watch now sends a keepalive checkpoint before a same-origin route link leaves the current Watch route. Previously, a client-side Next navigation could leave an ordinary account write pending without firing `pagehide`; if that write then failed, the newest checkpoint stayed on device and did not reach the member's other devices. A deterministic browser flow holds the first progress request, advances the paused video to a newer position, returns to the title and confirms the keepalive write stores that newest position and exposes `Continue watching` after reload. The flow plus the existing cross-context resume and paused-sync recovery checks pass 3/3; WatchPlayback component tests pass 69/69, web typecheck passes, and changed-source ESLint reports only the existing missing Pages-directory warning. This is local API fixture evidence; production PostgreSQL integration remains open.

Capture: .impeccable/review/settings/notification-retry-320x720.png. These checks use local identity and notification fixtures, not a production member.

## Advertising disclosure and cross-tab preferences — local, 2026-10-30

## Google sign-in and sign-up — local implementation, 2026-10-30

Sign-in and account creation now offer Google. The API uses authorization code, state, nonce and PKCE checks; validates Google's signed identity token; stores a unique Google subject on the member; and issues Consumit's existing opaque session. New members choose a username and accept the Terms and Privacy Policy. Google-authoritative Gmail/Workspace addresses can sign in or link to an existing verified member; other addresses complete Consumit's email verification. Pending authorization and signup claims are short-lived server-side state, and Google tokens are not persisted. The callback query is omitted from API and Nginx logs. The additive `20261030120000_google_oauth_identity` migration adds the provider subject. The Google action remains unavailable until `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` and `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` are configured and the OAuth client allows `${APP_ORIGIN}/v1/auth/google/callback`; apply the migration through the existing database deployment process. Static source and whitespace review completed; automated checks were not run for this slice.

## Watch keeps the current recording through URL renewal — local, 2026-10-30

When a timer renews the signed URL for the same healthy source and recording, Watch leaves the current media attached so playback, captions, and a paused position are not interrupted; the session keeps the newest lease for recovery. Returning from a hidden tab attaches a fresh URL for a direct file source and restores its saved position and play state. HLS stays attached to preserve its current buffered media, and the Relay HLS loader can also adopt the renewed capability in place. A changed provider, source ID, recording version, media type, or delivery path attaches the replacement. Cast follows its existing recovery path. WatchPlayback tests pass 72/72; the full Watch playback browser file passes 20/20 in Chrome and Chrome-touch. Relay HLS and cast behavior remain covered by their existing tests. This is deterministic local playback evidence, not a claim about every provider’s lease behavior.
